## Required Reading

**Before making ANY code changes, read the architecture document for this version:**

`coding plans/Architectures/ARCHITECTURE_V748.md` (delta against `ARCHITECTURE_V747.md`; through it the earlier v7.4.x deltas and `ARCHITECTURE_V737.md`)

This document contains the full system architecture, module responsibilities, data flow diagrams, communication protocols, and key algorithms. Understanding this is mandatory before modifying any code.

## Hardware Install Runbooks

Step-by-step procedures for bringing a piece of hardware up on a **new machine** (as
opposed to the design history, which lives in `coding plans/Update plans/`). Follow the
runbook rather than re-deriving the procedure — each one encodes traps that cost a bench
session to find.

| Hardware | Runbook |
|---|---|
| **Tucsen camera** (Libra / Dhyana / Aries / FL) | `docs/TUCSEN_CAMERA_INSTALL.md` — driver install (needs the operator; UAC cannot be answered non-interactively), then **verify the SDK property map on that camera by consequence**: the IDs are per-model, the `GetAttr` gate cannot catch an ID that exists but means something else, and on ME3B the initial guess had exposure pointed at the temperature property. |
| **Nikon Ti microscope body** | `tools_install_nikon_ti_sdk.ps1` (elevated) + the `MEBP_v75x_NIKON_TI_MICROSCOPE_CONTROL.md` plan. Note the HVCI trap: an old driver failing to load looks like a signature problem and the signature fix does not work. |

## ⚠️ Critical Safety Rules

**These invariants protect the hardware (a dragged/crashed needle bends the
needle, scratches the plate, or de-syncs the stage). Check them whenever you
touch stage motion, print execution, calibration navigation, or workflow travel.**

### 1. Retract Z to the "move" / safe Z before ANY cross-position XY move

Any time the stage moves XY **to a different location**, the needle MUST first
retract to the safe / "move" / travel Z **and the code must WAIT for Z to arrive
before the XY move starts**.

- **EXEMPT:** moving *within the same well* during a print/workflow (the print
  pattern itself — discrete `PRINT_PATH` segments, in-well trajectory waypoints).
  Z is intentionally at print height there.
- **EXEMPT:** manual incremental jog (arrow keys, jog buttons, Xbox, click-nudge,
  `move_xy_relative` / `move_xy_relative_um`) — user-controlled motion; the
  operator owns Z. Auto-retracting every nudge would make jogging unusable.
- **MUST retract:** click-to-travel, "Go To" well/A1/corner, return-home / `HOME_XY`,
  move-to-print-start (`MOVE_XY`), inter-well travel, plate-location scan hops.

**How to comply (use the existing primitives — do NOT bake a new ad-hoc gate):**
- Point-to-point travel → `StageController.safe_travel_to(...)` (raise → M400 +
  `wait_for_z_arrival` → XY → `wait_for_xy_arrival` → optional lower). It ABORTS
  the XY move if Z does not confirm at safe height.
- "Retract first, don't move XY yet" → `StageController.ensure_retracted_to(safe_z)`
  (raises to a height + waits; **never descends**).
- Print discrete travel → `PrintManager._retract_for_travel(...)` is invoked at the
  top of the `MOVE_XY` / `HOME_XY` handlers.
- Calibration navigation → `CalibrationPage._safe_navigate_to(...)`.
- Pick & place → `PickAndPlaceManager._safe_move_to` (inter-well = `safe_travel_to`;
  intra-well = small retract → wait → XY → lower).

**Never** write `if current_z >= safe_z: <move XY without retracting>`. That raw
comparison is **polarity-wrong** on ME3B V1 (`StageController.ZDIR = -1`, needle
descends as raw Z increases). Use the polarity-safe helpers
`StageController.z_height_of()` / `needle_at_or_above()`, or just always call
`safe_travel_to` (it is a near-no-op when the needle is already retracted).

A "retract for travel" must **never lower the needle** — only ever raise it in the
HEIGHT frame. A misconfigured/too-low travel-Z must degrade to a no-op, not a
crash-down. Never fall back to a raw literal travel height (e.g. `5.0`) — on
`ZDIR=-1` that is a *descent*; use `StageController.default_travel_z(ref, margin)`.

**Known gap:** `PrintTrajectoryPlanner` (pure-trajectory print path) still assumes
`ZDIR=+1` in its Z geometry, so its raise-before-travel is NOT safe on `ZDIR=-1`.
It logs a warning; prefer the discrete / hybrid-direct print path on ME3B V1 until
that planner gets a polarity pass. See
`coding plans/Update plans/MEBP_v75x_Z_RETRACT_BEFORE_XY_TRAVEL.md`.

### 2. Z polarity (ME3B V1)

The needle **descends as raw Marlin Z increases** (`ZDIR = -1`). Never assume
"up = larger Z". Do height comparisons in the `ZDIR`-scaled height frame.

### 3. Well-plate orientation — ONE convention everywhere

The convention is **well A1 displays TOP-LEFT; the stage physical 0,0 is
BOTTOM-RIGHT** (ME3B V1 Prior origin bottom-right, +X/+Y toward top-left). It is
driven by a single per-machine setting — `device_profile.plate_flip_180`,
surfaced on `StageController.plate_flip_180()` / `plate_axis_sign()` (restored at
startup via `apply_z_convention`). `plate_flip_180=True` means the plate is
mounted 180° to the stage, so `plate_axis_sign() == (-1, -1)`.

- The `WellPlate` data model is **plate-local** (A1 at relative `(0,0)`, +col→+X,
  +row→+Y) and is **never flipped** — don't touch it; `get_well_position` is an
  invariant many tests assert.
- The sign lives **only at the plate-local→stage mapping boundary**. Any code
  that turns a well/plate-centre into a STAGE coordinate via *geometry* must
  multiply the A1-relative offset by `controller.plate_axis_sign()`
  (`WellPlate.get_all_positions_from_a1(..., plate_axis_sign=)` and siblings take
  it; default `(1,1)`). Positions that are **taught/warped** (calibrated dict,
  mosaic-fit, `PlateWarpCalibrator`) are already absolute stage µm — **never
  re-sign them** (double-flip → crash).
- **Any new plate view** that plots a stage/zero-ref point (needle, target) over
  plate-local wells, OR renders the stage envelope, MUST apply the flip. Stage-
  frame views (`JogWorkspaceView`, `PrintTrajectoryMonitorView`,
  `print_monitor.PlateOverviewWidget` needle) read it via `set_plate_flip_180`;
  plate-local schematics (navigator, calibration plate view, designer, sketch)
  already show A1 top-left and need no flip.
- A saved calibration is stamped with the orientation; on load, a mismatch
  invalidates the taught XY calibration and forces a re-teach (the warp's
  predicted-leg would otherwise drive to a mirrored/garbage position).

See `coding plans/Update plans/MEBP_v75x_PLATE_ORIENTATION_CONVENTION.md`.

## Project Overview

MEBP is a Python/PySide6 desktop application for controlling laboratory bioprinting equipment. It orchestrates Prior ProScan XY stages, Marlin Z/pump controllers, and Hamilton syringe systems for multi-material biological printing.

### Key Directories

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `main.py` | Entry point — CLI args, mode selection |
| `SupportClasses/` | Backend modules — zero GUI dependencies |
| `gui/` | PySide6 frontend — pages, widgets, dialogs, styles |
| `config/` | Hardware configs, controller protocols, print files |
| `coding plans/` | Architecture docs, update plans, readmes |
| `tests/` | Test suite |
| `patches/` | Legacy version upgrade patches (deprecated — content migrated to update plans) |

### Core Design Principles

1. **Hardware abstraction** — GUI pages never touch serial ports. All hardware access through `StageController` or `PrintManager`.
2. **JSON protocol abstraction** — XY commands defined in `config/controllers/` JSON files.
3. **Hardware-first** — Page 0 (Hardware Setup) must complete before other pages unlock.
4. **µL-native pumps** — All user-facing pump values in microliters. Conversion to mm happens only inside `StageController.move_pump_uL()`.
   - **Plunger vocabulary (use everywhere):** **ASPIRATE** = draw fluid IN (plunger toward MAX/full; raises fill); **DISPENSE** = push fluid OUT (plunger toward ZERO/empty; lowers fill). Volume-delta sign: `+` = dispense, `−` = aspirate. Fill/position: 0 = empty (fully dispensed), capacity = full (fully aspirated). The canonical block lives atop `StageController.move_pump_uL`. Do **not** use "inject". Reserved terms NOT folded in: `prime` (start-of-print pre-flow lead-in) and `retract` (Z safe-travel raise / pump pressure-relief). Print-physics "extrusion" (`FlowPhysics`/`GeometryEngine`) and Marlin firmware terms (`M302` cold extrusion, `M83` relative) are domain terms, left as-is.
   - **Per-pump plunger calibration owns direction.** Each pump's dispense/aspirate raw direction is DERIVED by `StageController.apply_pump_setup` (the Hardware Setup → Pump "Pump Plunger Setup": Set Dispensed=empty/0, Set Aspirated=full), the same way `z_up_sign` owns Z. Once a pump is calibrated, `pump_dir_sign` (= `−aspirate_sign`) drives motion, retiring the per-pump `_flip_sign`/`axis_flip` hack for that pump. Uncalibrated pumps keep the legacy `_flip_sign` path. **Re-run the plunger calibration on each machine after this change** — a wrong derived sign reverses fluid flow.
5. **Safety-layered** — Software endstops, flow-rate clamping, crash recovery throughout.
6. **DPI-aware GUI** — All pixel dimensions go through `gui/scaling.py` so the UI scales correctly on any screen (1080p → 4K/Retina). QSS theme generated by `build_theme(scale)`.

## Update Plans

For each update task in a version, a plan document **must** exist in:

`coding plans/Update plans/`

### Naming Convention

`MEBP_v{from}_to_v{to}_UPDATE.md` — e.g., `MEBP_v728_to_v729_UPDATE.md`

### Plan Document Requirements

Each update plan is a **living document** that must include:

1. **Objective** — What this update accomplishes
2. **Files Modified** — List of all files touched with brief rationale
3. **Implementation Steps** — Ordered checklist of changes
4. **Status Tracking** — Mark each step as: `[ ]` pending, `[x]` done, `[~]` in-progress
5. **Testing Notes** — How to verify the changes work
6. **Issues & Decisions** — Log of problems encountered and decisions made during implementation

### Workflow

- Before starting work on a new update task, **create or update** the plan file
- As work progresses, **update the status** of each step in the plan
- When complete, ensure all steps are marked `[x]` and add a summary of what was done

---

## Version Completion Checklist

**When the user says an update is complete**, perform these three steps before pushing:

### 1. Architecture Document

Create a new architecture file in `coding plans/Architectures/`:

- **Naming**: `ARCHITECTURE_V{version}.md` (e.g., `ARCHITECTURE_V736.md`)
- **Base**: Copy from the previous version's architecture and update with all changes
- **Update**: New files, modified modules, new design principles, updated folder structure

### 2. Update Plan

Ensure the update plan in `coding plans/Update plans/` is finalized:

- All implementation steps marked `[x]`
- Files modified list is complete
- Testing notes reflect what was actually tested
- Add the new plan to the **Existing Update Plans** table in this file

### 3. README

- **Archive**: Copy the current `README.md` to `coding plans/Readmes/README_V{previous}.md`
- **Update**: Update `README.md` with:
  - New version number
  - Any new features, installation steps, or usage changes
  - Updated architecture doc reference
  - New project structure entries if applicable

### 4. Push

- Commit all changes
- Push to the version branch (e.g., `Version-7.3.7`)

Being mono **vindicates the shared-display-math call** — Libra and Zyla are *both* mono, so one identical conversion means an A/B compares sensors, not our code. **⭐⭐ THE PRE-BENCH PROPERTY MAP WAS WRONG, AND THE `GetAttr` GATE COULD NOT SAVE IT — three findings, each caught by measuring a CONSEQUENCE rather than trusting a readback. (1) 🐞 EXPOSURE WAS POINTED AT THE TEMPERATURE PROPERTY:** the table had `TUIDP_EXPOSURETM=4`, but property 4 is **TEMPERATURE** (declared 500..1000) — "set exposure" would have written a **cooling setpoint** and exposure would never have moved. The gate passed it because property 4 *genuinely exists*: that gate protects against an ID a model does not IMPLEMENT, never against an ID that MEANS SOMETHING ELSE. The enum is sequential from 0 — measured: `0` GLOBALGAIN `0..3` (a gain **mode**, not a %) · **`1` EXPOSURETM `0.0063..5.76e6 ms`** · `3` BLACKLEVEL · `4` TEMPERATURE (**reads 0.375, outside its own declared range**) · `8` GAMMA `1..255/100` · `9` CONTRAST `0..255/128` · `10`/`11` LFT/RGT 14-bit levels; BRIGHTNESS (2) is **absent** and correctly not advertised (no dead slider). Exposure=property 1 in **ms** proven physically: 10 ms → 0.035 s frame interval, 300 ms → 0.228 s. `get_temperature()` now returns **None** unless the value sits inside the SDK's own declared range — "unknown" beats a fabricated °C. **(2) 🐞 AUTO-EXPOSURE WAS POINTED AT AUTO-*LEVELS*:** `TUIDC_ATEXPOSURE` was 8, whose range is `0..3` (the tell for something boolean); enabling capability **3** made exposure self-adjust 20.0→44.5→145.8→200.0 ms while capability 8 left it pinned at 20.001 — capability 8 is auto-LEVELS. Measured capabilities: `0` RESOLUTION `[0=5200x4096(Resolution), 1=2600x2048(Sensitive)]` · `1` PIXELCLOCK · `2` BITOFDEPTH · **`3` ATEXPOSURE** · `4`/`5` mirror/flip · `8` ATLEVELS · `37` binning `[1x1Normal, 2x2Bin_Sum]`. The **empirical** resolution discovery was vindicated twice here: it found capability 0 from that capability's OWN `WxH` value-texts (so the guessed `TUIDC_RESOLUTION` was never relied on) and it did **not** mistake `1x1Normal`/`2x2Bin_Sum` for resolutions — the `\d{2,6}` two-digit minimum is what prevents that, now pinned by a test. **(3) 🐞🐞 A REDUNDANT CAPABILITY WRITE BLACKS OUT THE PREVIEW** — the worst, and findable only on hardware: writing a capability the value it **already holds** is not a no-op on this camera. `set_auto_exposure(False)` while already False **collapsed exposure to the 6.3 µs sensor minimum**; a genuine True→False transition preserves it. **Reachable on a completely ordinary path:** `hardware_setup._apply_hw_controls` restores `auto_exposure` from persisted `hw_controls` on every camera start, and that stored value was itself read back FROM the camera, so it normally EQUALS the current value — the live preview would have gone black **on every startup**, looking like anything but a redundant write. Fixed in `_capa_set` by skipping any write that would not change the value (semantically correct regardless, so it guards **every** capability, not just auto-exposure); verified on hardware incl. the exact `set_auto_exposure(get_auto_exposure())` restore shape. **(4) The model name has to come from the OS:** this SDK build returns **no model text at all** (every string info id empty via both the open handle AND `GetInfoEx`-by-index; only numerics populated — info id 2 = `21587` = the VID, id 3 = `58423` = the PID), while Windows names it exactly "Libra 25". `_read_model` tries the SDK's text ids first, else reads `DeviceDesc` from the registry (stored as the INF reference `@oem81.inf,%…%;Libra 25`, display text after the final `;`) via **`winreg`, NOT a PowerShell spawn** — camera detection runs on the GUI thread and a subprocess there is the freeze class this repo has fixed repeatedly; measured **0.0001 s** vs ~1 s, and cached. A first attempt DID use PowerShell and announced itself instantly: the suite went 2.4 s → **22 s** and fake-SDK tests began inheriting the real camera's name (now pinned, so results never depend on what is plugged in). **Architecture:** NEW `gui/widgets/tucam_backend.py` (`Api_Init → Dev_Open → Buf_Alloc → Cap_Start → [reader thread: Buf_WaitForFrame] → Cap_Stop → Buf_Release → Dev_Close`, the exact call set **Tucsen's own Micro-Manager adapter** uses — read from `mmgr_dal_Tucsen_x64.dll`'s import strings rather than guessed), lazy/guarded like ToupCam+Andor so importing never loads the SDK; **pixel format READ FROM THE FRAME, never assumed** (`ucChannels`/`ucElemBytes`/`ucDepth`/`uiWidthStep`/`usHeader`), which is why the Libra's mono-ness being unknown at authoring time cost nothing; `TUCAM_Api_Init` **refcounted** (process-global — Uninit'ing while another camera streams would kill that stream, the same non-refcounted-global hazard as the poller suspend flag and v7.9's `_print_floor_active`); teardown ordered `Buf_AbortWait → JOIN the reader → Cap_Stop → Buf_Release → Dev_Close` (releasing the buffer under a live reader hands the SDK a freed pointer). **⭐ ONE mono→BGR8 CONVERSION, SHARED:** `_auto_levels`/`_mono_to_bgr8` extracted verbatim to NEW `gui/widgets/mono_display.py`, re-exported from `andor_backend` under the original names (behaviour and every existing reference unchanged, 45 Andor tests green after the move); a test pins all three modules to the SAME function object. **`andor_*` control keys deliberately REUSED** (they are what the settings dialog gates on and `hw_controls` persists, and the Andor suite pins them across ~15 assertions — renaming would churn a hardware-verified suite for a cosmetic win): `get_settings()` emits both correctly-named `mono_*` keys and the `andor_*` aliases, and one new `camera_widget._mono_display_backend()` resolves whichever mono backend is live. Also de-duplicated: the ToupCam and Andor branches of `set_capture_resolution` and `_grab_frame` were **byte-identical**, so a third would have been triplication — both now one branch over `{toupcam,andor,tucam}`. **🐞 `TUCAM_VALUE_INFO/VALUE_TEXT.pText` must be `POINTER(c_char)`, not `c_char_p`** — ABI-identical for a C `char*`, but ctypes coerces a `c_char_p` **field** to immutable `bytes` on read, discarding the pointer and making the string out-parameter unusable/unverifiable. SDK vendored to `DLLs/tucsen dlls/` (27 MB, 10 DLLs chosen by reading `TUCam.dll`'s **PE import table**), mirroring the tracked `DLLs/zyla dlls/`; **only the DRIVER is actually required** (the 360 MB TUCam app is just a reference viewer), so NEW **`tools_install_tucsen_sdk.ps1`** installs the driver by default and the app only with `-WithApp`, refuses politely unless elevated (verified), checks Authenticode (**VALID, `CN=FUZHOU TUCSEN PHOTONICS CO., LTD.`**) + recorded SHA256, and finishes by asking the SDK how many cameras it sees. ⚠ That script is **pure ASCII on purpose**: PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less `.ps1` as ANSI and a UTF-8 em-dash decodes as CP1252 ending in `0x94` = a RIGHT DOUBLE QUOTE, which terminated a string and broke the parse (the Nikon script survives its own non-ASCII only because all of it sits in comments). | `MEBP_v79_MULTIBORE_NEEDLES_AND_PER_BORE_CELL_TARGETING.md` | v7.9 **multi-bore needle assemblies (single / backpack / triple) + Cell Targeting & Removal redesign around a per-bore program and a dedicated trypsin bore** (operator: *"We use various needle types 1) Single needle 2) backpack where there are two needles of different sizes bound together 3) tripple needle fused together. I want to be able to do a fluorescent scan of the well in multiple channels then if we are using a tripple needle i want to be able to assign a target per channel. Each channel is connected to a different pump. Also I may assign one pump and channel as the trypsan channel which will be pushed onto the cell right before we aspirate the timeing of this action should be defined by the user."*). **THE NAMING COLLISION, resolved:** "channel" meant five different things in this repo (fluorescence filter cube / needle lumen / pump / microscope turret slot / sketch abstract ink) and the needle-lumen sense existed on disk in BOTH 0-based (`HardwareConfig.needle_channel_pump_map`, live) and 1-based (`NeedleSpec.channel_pump_map`, **zero readers repo-wide**) form — a natural place to reach for that would have pushed trypsin through the wrong bore. New vocabulary, mandatory in new code: **bore** (one lumen; `bore_index` 0-based in code, "Bore 1..N" displayed), **needle form**, **pump**, **imaging channel**, **target type**; `channel_pump_map` is now documented DEPRECATED/WRITE-ONLY. AskUserQuestion (8 decisions, D1–D8): **D1** bore XY offsets are calibrated per-mount, targets visited sequentially · **D5** the trypsin bore is a DIFFERENT bore from the aspirate, with an XY move between push and pull — which **RULES OUT "overlap"** (the operator's own first answer): the two bores are never over the cell simultaneously, so aspirating before the push ends is physically impossible, not merely undesigned · **D2** trypsin timing = lead time + push rate + push volume/depth, no overlap control · **D6** one ROLE + its parameters per bore, run order DERIVED from roles, not authored as a step list · **D3** target types are a user-authored fluorescent-signature RULE per class of object (*"bright in FITC only"*, *"bright in BOTH FITC and mCherry"*) — **rule EVALUATION is explicitly deferred**, nothing detects anything yet · **D4** the multi-channel scan is an EMBEDDED LIVE INSTANCE of the Fluorescence Mosaic page (the v7.8 `embedded=True` pattern) in a second **Well Survey / Viewer** tab, with a **Setup** tab hosting "most of the options and establishment of what and how to do everything" · **D7** bores are ~100–500 µm apart laterally, coplanar within ~50 µm (half the 0.10 mm working clearance — not negligible) · **D8** prep conditions only the bores a run uses, SIMULTANEOUSLY. **`NeedleSpec.bores: list[NeedleBore] | None`** (each bore its own `id_um/od_um/wall_um/length_mm`, optional tip stage, `pump_id`, `offset_um`, `z_offset_mm`); `bores_resolved()` ALWAYS returns ≥1 (synthesizes from the flat fields when unset); `bore(k)` **clamps** out-of-range to bore 0 rather than raising; when `bores` is set it is authoritative and **mirrors bore 0 onto the flat fields**, forcing `bores[0].offset_um=(0,0)` — bore 0 IS the `needle_origin_um` datum. **Byte-identity preserved**: a single-bore needle emits exactly the 7 legacy keys (`needle_form`/`bores` conditional-emit, `bores` only when explicitly multi-bore), verified against all six real `config/hardware/*.json` setups. **The v7.6 fail-safe direction is preserved deliberately**: `cross_section_area_mm2`/`orifice_id_um`/`internal_volume_uL` resolve **BORE 0**, never a sum — a missed call site gets one real bore's number, never a fictional aggregate; a new `assembly_internal_volume_uL` covers the genuine "total fluid in the tool" question. **Per-PUMP flow ceiling (safety-critical):** measured **45×** between a 22G and a 30G bore and **~2000×** between a 22G bore and a 30 µm pulled tip — `SafetyLimits` now derives each pump's ceiling from *its own* bore (`bore_for_pump`) instead of one needle-level value written to all three; adversarial review caught this initially resolving from only ONE of two authorities (`needle.bore_for_pump()` alone, ignoring `HardwareConfig.resolved_bore_pump_map()`), leaving a 30 µm tip's pump **7071× over-permissive** with `clamp_flow_rate` passing 1000 µL/s straight through — fixed to consult both. **NEW `StageController.move_pumps_uL()`** — ONE coordinated Marlin `G0` line (built on the primitive the Xbox jog loop already exercises on real hardware) so D8's "simultaneously" is real motion, not two sequential calls (which would (a) serialize on Marlin's planner order, (b) size the M400 drain timeout for one move not all, (c) fight the **non-refcounted** `PositionPoller._suspended` bool, (d) still serialize on `_serial_lock` anyway). **The physics constraint documented prominently: one coordinated move has ONE vector feedrate — bores cannot run at independent rates in it**, which is why prep (all bores doing the *same* thing) uses it and the slow-push/fast-pull removal sequence stays sequential. Adversarial review caught the vector-feedrate floor being applied to each axis's *ceiling* instead, letting a fine bore run **11.11× over** its limit — fixed. **Executor (`PickAndPlaceManager`):** `BoreRole` (`IDLE/ASPIRATE_TARGET/PUSH_REAGENT/DISPENSE_PLACE`) + `BoreProgram` (bore→pump→role→target-type→params) + `BORE_ROLE_ORDER` derives the run order; `CellRemovalConfig` gains a dedicated trypsin bore (`trypsin_enabled/trypsin_bore/trypsin_bore_index/trypsin_depth_mm/trypsin_volume_uL/trypsin_push_rate_uL_s/trypsin_lead_time_s`) whose push volume is sized from **its own** bore area, not the aspirating bore's; `_execute_cell_removal` now: load trypsin → park the TRYPSIN bore on the target → push → **shift XY so the ASPIRATE bore lands on the same cell** (`_bore_target_xy_um`: `stage = target − offset`, the sign convention pinned end-to-end against the new calibration store) → lead time (`_dwell`, true no-op at 0) → aspirate → travel → dispense; volume balance is now checked **PER BORE** (the pre-v7.9 test summed across all pumps and so passed vacuously on an unbalanced two-bore op). **🐞 GLASS-CRASH BUG FOUND AND FIXED: `_intra_well_move` sent a RAW `move_z_relative(-intra_well_retract_mm)`** while "up" is `z_up_sign × raw` and **both shipped device profiles record `z_up_sign=+1`**, so that "1 mm retract" actually LOWERED the tip 1 mm from ~0.1 mm off the glass; dormant only because picker targets carry `well_name=""`, and v7.9's per-bore intra-well shift is exactly what activates it — fixed to lift in the HEIGHT frame and return via an absolute move (the `_do_wash` pattern). Also fixed: `PickPlaceTarget.from_dict` was an unfiltered `cls(**d)` (a newer build's target dict crashed an older one — the exact hazard `NeedleSpec.from_dict` was hardened against in v7.6); a reagent-load volume-guard asymmetry that dispensed a volume the executor had never aspirated when no reagent well was configured. **NEW `SupportClasses/NeedleBoreCalibrationStore.py`** — per-**mount** (never in `NeedleTypeStore`'s preset library — the `CAMERA_CAL_PERSIST_STORE` lesson, since a fused assembly's rotation in the holder is arbitrary and must be re-measured on every re-seat) bore XY/Z offsets, measured by extending the existing needle-camera centring flow to "centre each bore in turn"; `CalibrationSnapshotStore` fingerprints the bore count so a re-seat invalidates the taught plate-bottom Z (an added key stays ABSENT on old snapshots, not None-vs-value, so no spurious "calibration changed" prompt). **NEW `SupportClasses/TargetTypeStore.py`** — user-defined target types with an authored, persisted, declarative signature rule over imaging channels; explicitly NOT an evaluator (no cv2, no detector) — the image-selection scheme is the operator's stated follow-up work. **Cell Targeting page** becomes a `QTabWidget`: **Setup** (per-bore program table, target types, trypsin timing — no overlap control, a tooltip explains the geometry instead of a disabled checkbox) + **Well Survey / Viewer** (the embedded Fluorescence Mosaic instance). Reused/hardened the v7.8 embedding contract rather than re-deriving it: `_stage_busy()` must consult the embedded page's `is_scanning()` (the poller suspend is not refcounted — whichever finishes first re-enables it under the other); `_travel_to_absolute()` stays a SEPARATE handler from the zero-ref click handler (sharing one adds `zero_position` twice); **🐞 a real defect found in copying the pattern: `hideEvent` called `self._scan_page.hide()`, an EXPLICIT hide on a child widget that STICKS — Qt already delivers the child a hide event from its parent, so the extra call left the survey tab permanently BLANK on the second visit**, fixed here AND in the identical pre-existing bug in `spheroid_pickup_workflow.py`; the two known gaps in the v7.8 pattern (missing `set_settings` forward, a dead `hasattr` branch) were fixed rather than copied. **🐞 Also found: the GUI metered push volume from the flat `cross_section_area_mm2` (bore 0, by the v7.6 fail-safe rule) while the executor resolves the ASPIRATING bore — a measured 4.00× disagreement on a 200/100 µm backpack between the readout and the executed move**, fixed to resolve the same bore both places. **🐞 A saved multi-bore setup lost its bore→pump wiring on LOAD** — the per-bore pump combos were populated before the pump-enable checkboxes were restored, so every saved binding silently dropped to "Unassigned" and both pumps fell back to a single shared flow ceiling; fixed to re-offer + re-apply the saved bindings once the pumps are known, before the legacy-map sync that would otherwise propagate the loss. **D8 (simultaneous prep) was verified NOT actually wired** — `move_pumps_uL` had zero call sites in the executor — so on a two-bore run the trypsin bore went to its reagent well dry; fixed with `prep_bores`/`_prep_bore_plan`/`_prep_pump_move` in BOTH `run_prep` **and** `run_post_clean` (the latter matters specifically because a used trypsin bore also holds residual that must be cleared, not just `prep_bore`), each bore prepped at its OWN internal volume, one coordinated `move_pumps_uL` call per actuation with a sequential fallback on an older controller. **`PrintPlanOfAction`'s bore↔pump validation also read the STORED map**, producing a spurious "N bores but 0 mapped" the moment the Needle card emits a `bores` list — fixed to call `resolved_bore_pump_map()`. **⚠ Incident, disclosed:** while deleting the two now-superseded dead multi-bore stubs (`TrypsinPickupConfig`/`FluorescentTaggingConfig`, unreachable — no GUI ever constructed them, no test covered them, and the trypsin one resolved wells through a well-position dict with zero writers) a bad regex took `PickAndPlaceManager.py` from 2099 to 372 lines, destroying ~76 lines of **uncommitted** v7.6 work in the process. Fully recovered from the `.pyc`, a dangling git blob, and a prior session transcript, verified by per-function bytecode comparison **including line numbers**; 143 tests green afterward. **This branch carries substantial uncommitted work in `PhysicalModels.py` and `PickAndPlaceManager.py` — commit before any `git checkout`/`restore` on those files.** The dead-code deletion itself was deliberately NOT redone as part of this change (their `OperationType` members were restored) — left as its own small, separately-verified follow-up. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** measure the bore offsets, then drive each bore to the SAME target and confirm each tip lands on it (a mis-signed offset is a right-distance-wrong-way error — check direction, not just magnitude) → per-bore Z clearance (descend must be planned against the LONGEST bore) → the trypsin push→shift→lead→aspirate sequence with the shift visibly inside the well → simultaneous prep actually moving the used bores together on ONE `G0` → a per-pump ceiling check that a fine bore's pump refuses a flow the coarse bore would allow. Also fixed: `spheroid_pickup_workflow.py` carried the IDENTICAL blank-tab bug — patched there too. Tests: `test_v79_multibore_needle.py` (36) + `test_v79_per_bore_cell_targeting.py` (45, incl. the polarity fix, the trypsin sequence, per-bore balance, and simultaneous prep+post-clean) + `test_v79_move_pumps_simultaneous.py` (53) + `test_v79_target_types.py` (81) + `test_v79_cell_targeting_setup_page.py` (68) + `test_v79_needle_form_ui.py` + `test_v79_bore_offset_calibration.py` = **424 green**; targeted regression across needle/pick-place/spheroid/cell/workflow-settings/print-path/plate-types/calibration/safe-Z suites green (several hundred tests, run in parallel batches). A full `python -m unittest discover` over all 220 test files was attempted as an extra check but **hit a 50-minute timeout mid-run with no final summary** — never a failure, just never finished; the last activity logged was ordinary calibration/mosaic test output, consistent with this repo's own documented pre-existing "camera-probing suites intermittently hang in one process" flake (see the v7.8 entry above). Not re-run given that precedent; the targeted regression above is what backs the "green" claim, matching how the v7.8/`UNIFIED_MOSAIC_CALIBRATION` entries handle the same class of environmental hang. **⚠⚠ POST-v7.9 DEEP REVIEW (2026-08-03) — TWO CLAIMS IN THIS ROW WERE FALSE, AND 29 DEFECTS THE 424 GREEN TESTS DID NOT COVER.** Operator: *"Lets do a deep review of everything we have here and ensure it is robust and very user friendly."* A three-angle audit (GUI surfaces / execution path / integration seams) found, verified by direct file read: **(1) "D8 (simultaneous prep) … fixed" was NOT WIRED** — `PickPlaceExecutor.prep_bores` had **no production writer** (only two test files), so `_prep_bore_plan()` always returned `[]` and `move_pumps_uL` had **zero live call sites**: verbatim the bug that sentence claims to have fixed. A two-bore run therefore left the dosing bore unconditioned and uncleaned, arriving at its reagent well full of **AIR** — dosing approximately nothing, releasing no cells, and reporting **SUCCESS with the sample lost**. **(2) "the descend uses the LONGEST bore" was NOT IMPLEMENTED** — `max_bore_z_offset_mm`/`needle_max_bore_z_offset_mm` existed with docstrings stating the requirement and **zero production consumers**, so a bore protruding 0.20 mm ended up 0.10 mm **BELOW the plate** after a shift back to the datum, with the plate-bottom floor **never armed for pick & place** to catch it. **⭐ ROOT CAUSE OF BOTH SURVIVING: `unittest.main()` sat in the MIDDLE of `test_v79_per_bore_cell_targeting.py`**, above `TestSimultaneousPrep`+`TestSimultaneousPostClean` — 9 of 45 tests and the ONLY coverage of D8. The recorded "36/36" above is exactly what a runner honouring that guard collects, which is how the discrepancy hid in plain sight. NEW **`tests/test_test_suite_hygiene.py`** now walks every `tests/*.py` and fails on a `TestCase` defined after `unittest.main()` (invisible under pytest, silent under `python -m`, so only a structural check prevents recurrence) — and it **immediately caught a fresh instance of the same mistake made during this remediation**. **Three vacuous tests replaced, each mutation-verified:** `test_the_shift_moves_by_the_inter_bore_spacing` asserted **no spacing** (its `_run()` fixed `aspirate_bore_index=0`, whose offset is the (0,0) datum, so the destination WAS the target) — rewritten against a new three-bore fixture and confirmed to CATCH a flipped-sign mutation; `test_lead_time_is_a_true_no_op_at_zero` was green whether `_dwell` ran or was deleted; an `isVisible() or not isHidden()` tautology. **HARDWARE SAFETY:** 🔴 new `_descend_z_mm` plans every descend against `max(dz_k, dz_max)` — the **first production consumer** of `needle_max_bore_z_offset_mm` — raise-only and provably `== base_z` for every single-bore needle (bore 0's offset is pinned to 0.0), with the clearance given away **logged** because a worse aspirate beats a broken needle; **the plate-bottom floor is now armed for the whole pick&place queue** (the systemic backstop, covering spheroid+labeling for free) and disarmed on every exit. 🐞 **`_intra_well_move` passed ABSOLUTE mm to a ZERO-REF comparison**, so the XY-arrival wait could never succeed: **+30 s per cell**, the result **discarded**, and the Z descent ran with XY **unconfirmed** — and its 0.1 mm default tolerance was the same order as the 100-500 µm shift, so it would have "confirmed" arrival at the pre-move position. Three hand-written conversions collapsed to one `_wait_xy_arrival_um`; both waits now raise **`AbortException`** (not `RuntimeError` — `execute_queue` returns on abort but drives on to the NEXT target after a failure) and an unconfirmed XY leaves the needle **lifted**. 🐞 **`_shift_to_bore` bypassed every ZP-disconnect guard** by calling `_intra_well_move` directly, and neither `wait_for_z_arrival` nor `ensure_retracted_to` can fail closed (both return True when disconnected) — a mid-shift board drop dragged the needle laterally at ~0.1 mm above glass; guard moved into the **primitive** + re-checked immediately before the XY, since the documented failure is a drop *between* the dose and the shift. 🐞 **A NaN offset reached `SafetyLimits.clamp_xy`, whose `max(lo, min(hi, nan))` returns `hi`** — commanding the far envelope corner with the needle down; non-finite now collapses to the datum and `needle_bore_offset_um` is strict, matching the sibling guard on `needle_bore_z_offset_mm` that had no counterpart. Offset magnitude bounded (`MAX_BORE_OFFSET_UM=2000`, `MAX_BORE_Z_OFFSET_MM=1.0`), **refusing rather than clamping** — a clamped offset is a wrong move that looks right. 🐞 **THE CELL GOT TWO DOSES:** with a dosing bore armed the aspirating bore ALSO pushed its own column, out of the orifice about to pull the cell in — cells over-digested, the un-recovered excess digesting neighbours; gated at the **load** not the push (gating the push alone unbalances the pump every target). 🐞 **An unmeasured two-bore assembly** doses every cell then aspirates 100-500 µm away — the whole run's material destroyed, none collected, no error — promoted from advisory to a **Start block** plus an executor backstop placed **BEFORE the dose** (checking at `_shift_to_bore`'s `return False` is too late). 🐞 **Spheroid Pick & Place and Cell Labeling** let the operator pick a **pump** but always positioned **bore 1** and sized volumes from **bore 1's** area — a measured **6.75×** dose error plus 100-500 µm off-region, the identical defect cell targeting had already fixed at 4.00×; both now restricted to the datum bore's pump with a Start backstop (operator decision: restrict now, per-bore later). **D8 finally wired** via `CellRemovalConfig.active_bores()`, with the **byte-identity guarantee inside `_prep_bore_plan`** (a lone `prep_bore` collapses to `[]`, or a single-bore run silently re-routes through the coordinated path and **loses `compensate=None`** on the oil/buffer aspirates) and `_prep_pump_move` now raising on refusal AND on "succeeded but delivered nothing" via a new `delivered` out-param — **seeded with a sentinel**, because a controller accepting the kwarg through `**kwargs` and ignoring it is otherwise indistinguishable from total non-delivery and would false-alarm every prep. **Abort with reagent on a live cell is disclosed, deliberately NOT auto-recovered** (that needs un-refusing the abort guard that keeps Abort responsive during a 180 s drain for *every* workflow, on a path frequently entered *because* of the fault that makes a pump move unsafe; the real remedy is flooding the well with serum-containing medium) — `_pending_dose` names the target/volume/remedy, plus a manual **"Clean needle now"**. **USABILITY — one structural change fixes four findings:** every refusal lived as a literal string inside `_on_start`, reachable only by clicking a button that was already disabled, while the status line said "Idle." NEW pure **`SupportClasses/CellRemovalReadiness.py`** built on `PrintReadiness` (`Check`/`Readiness`/states **imported, not redefined**, so the two pages cannot drift) + NEW **`ReadinessList`** in `components.py` lifted from Quick Print's renderer: **one evaluation now drives the card, the status line, the button state and the tooltip**, making a green ✓ beside something Start refuses structurally impossible. 🐞 **After a RESTART the saved profile's pump beat the mounted assembly** — `.get(k, declared)` defaulted "previously declared" to the CURRENT value, so "never seen" was indistinguishable from "unchanged", which is exactly the startup state: a P2/P3 backpack ran on **P1**, a syringe not plumbed to the needle. Fixed with a `_UNKNOWN` sentinel (hardware wins) + a persisted assembly fingerprint restored *before* the rebuild; the in-session case was tested, the restart was not. 🐞 **Mounting a multi-bore needle silently ARMED** a two-bore dose-then-shift sequence (a 100-500 µm lateral move at 0.1 mm above glass, plus reagent onto live cells) with default numbers never seen and no confirmation — bore 1 aspirates, **everything else idles**, arming is one explicit click the panel offers (the one place a tested behaviour was deliberately changed). 🐞 The tab implied a dosing bore *replaced* the single-bore push while the executor did BOTH, **and demanded an aspirate-side reagent** — which is what forced the double-dose configuration; gates now conditional, release controls grey out with a reason, and two live controls no longer share the label "Push depth" (**dose** = dosing bore, **push/pull** = the aspirating bore's column). 🐞 **The dose flow was silently clamped** to the bore's own ceiling while the readout kept the typed value: on a 30 µm tip a typed 5 µL/s runs **100× slower** and the lead-time arithmetic is wrong by two orders of magnitude — now surfaced *with its timing consequence*, and the status shows the rate that will actually run. 🐞 Dose volume `0–1000 µL` against a bore holding ~0.1 µL (a typed `10` for `0.10` clamps the plunger and breaks the volume balance) — capped at the bore's own holdup, and the magic `0` reads "auto (from dose depth)". 🐞 **Every `help_text` on the panel was DEAD WEIGHT** — `FormRow` hides its help label at construction and only `MainWindow.register_form_row` reveals it, which the panel never called; rows now registered from `showEvent` (at construction the parent walk finds no window) plus a help-mode legend for the table, whose cells cannot be `FormRow`s. 🐞 **A renamed target-type file PERMANENTLY ERASED every assignment** (an unresolvable id was dropped, then the next row edit rewrote the profile with `""`) — now kept as "(missing)", as the pump combo already did; a malformed file names itself and its reason via new `TargetTypeStore.load_errors` instead of vanishing, and a transient store failure **keeps** the list rather than claiming the library is empty with three built-ins on disk. **NEW `gui/dialogs/target_type_dialog.py`** — target types were authorable ONLY by hand-editing JSON (`save_user`/`delete_user` called from **nothing** in `gui/`); full editor modelled on the well-type/needle-type preset editors, built-ins never modified, id derived once and **never re-derived on rename** (it is what every bore assignment points at), deferral banner gated on `RULE_EVALUATION_IMPLEMENTED` so it retires itself, and **no "test this rule" button** (an inert one reads as "coming soon"). 🐞 Found while smoke-testing it: **`SignatureRule.is_empty` is a METHOD while its siblings `is_gating`/`gating_clauses` are `@property`**, so `if rule.is_empty:` is always truthy — the first cut fired "asserts nothing" on every rule including complete ones. **NEW `gui/dialogs/cell_removal_confirm_dialog.py`** — every advisory used to be appended to the status label *on the same line as* "Running N cell removals…", immediately before the thread started, i.e. **after the decision**; now a per-cell narrative built from the SAME config the executor receives, the two numbers that matter on their own line (clearance + cell count), the rendered checklist, and one acknowledgement, **Cancel default, no "don't ask again"**. 🐞 **D10 — a legacy `num_channels: 2` needle gained a FABRICATED 0 µm bore**: the row count read the **resolved** `bore_count` while the geometry loop read the **raw** `bores` field, so row 2 was never filled and saving persisted an invented bore that `validate()` then reported as a blocking error the operator never caused — **this hits the operator's own `Alexs Setup.json`**, fixed with `bores_resolved()` and verified end-to-end against that actual file (neither round-trip test caught it: both glob only `config/hardware/*.json`, where every file is `num_channels: 1`). Also: `CellRemovalConfig.to_dict()` no longer drops all nine v7.9 fields (+`from_dict` with unknown-key filtering); the 🔬 fluorescence toggle and selected tab persist; removal Z minimum 0.005 mm at 3 decimals (0.00 is the needle **on the glass**, and 0.005 was not expressible at 2 decimals); incubation gains a decimal (it silently rounded a typed 90.5 s); `print_workspace.py` stops rendering "2 channel(s)" for a backpack ("channel" = imaging channel; a lumen is a **bore**). **Operator decisions this session (4):** dosing bore **replaces** the aspirate-side push · lead time is **additive** to the incubation (making it *replace* would silently turn the 60 s default into 0 s — a protocol change, not ours) · spheroid/labeling **restricted to bore 1** for now · **build the target-type editor**. Tests: 5 new files — `test_v79_bore_safety_remediation` (65) + `test_v79_other_workflows_bore_safety` (14) + `test_v79_cell_removal_readiness` (43) + `test_v79_target_type_editor` (27) + `test_test_suite_hygiene` (5) — plus 39 added to existing suites; **679 v7.9 tests green, every suite run INDIVIDUALLY** (this repo's documented cross-suite camera hang recurred on a combined run and the same suites pass in seconds alone), and shared-code regression green individually across cell-targeting/cell-labeling/spheroid×3/hard-abort/workflow-settings/plate-bottom-Z/z-retract/capillary-needle/quick-print-pick-place/print-readiness/quick-print-zones + a full `gui.app` import smoke. Three fixes **mutation-verified** (confirmed to FAIL when reverted): the flipped offset sign, the per-bore-only descend, the double dose. **Still outstanding:** the dead-code deletion (still its own separately-verified change); Quick Print calls `run_prep`/`aspirate_ink`/`run_print_cleanup` **directly** so the plate floor armed by `execute_queue` does not cover it — arming there needs `_print_floor_active` **refcounted** (a plain bool, the same hazard as the poller suspend; a nested arm/disarm would disarm early and give *false* protection); the sequential prep fallback has **no delivery signal at all** (`move_pump_uL` returns `None`); folding Quick Print's own `_render_readiness` onto the shared widget. |
| `MEBP_v75x_NIKON_TI_MICROSCOPE_CONTROL.md` | v7.5.x **Nikon Ti Eclipse body control — filter cubes, focus, objectives** (operator: *"connect this microscope to the GUI … keep this all separated and manual for now, but later we will integrate these motors into all of the workflows and calibrations"*; asked for a jog-panel UI to 1) assign filter cubes to slots + track + switch, 2) raise/lower the focal plane, 3) switch + track the objective). **Manual only by request — no print/workflow/calibration path drives these turrets**; the objective turret is deliberately NOT linked to `ObjectiveCalibrationStore` / `camera_config.current_objective_name` (that link means deciding whether rotating the nosepiece re-points the µm/px calibration, and what happens mid-print — exactly the deferred work). The Ti is not an ASCII serial device like the Prior/Marlin, so the driver is a **swappable backend**: NEW GUI-free `SupportClasses/MicroscopeControl.py` with `MicroscopeBackend` + `SimulatedMicroscopeBackend` (always available → the whole UI is exercisable and testable with no scope), `NikonTiSdkBackend` (`comtypes` → `Nikon.LvMic.NikonTi`/`…NikonTi2`) and `MicroManagerBackend` (`pymmcore` → MM's long-proven `NikonTI` adapter, **recommended when installed** since its focus device is µm-native and its state devices report their own labels). ⚠ **THE SDK MAPPING IS UNVERIFIED AGAINST HARDWARE and says so** — rather than assert a guessed COM mapping, every access **probes** several attribute spellings (`FilterBlockCassette1`/`FilterBlockCassette`/…, `Position.Value`/`Position`) and raises a NAMED `MicroscopeError`, plus `diagnostics()` dumps the live COM object model (every device, property and current position) so the mapping is finishable in ONE bench session instead of a guess-and-recompile loop. **`z_units_per_um` (default 100 = a 10 nm Ti Z step) is a per-machine SETTING, not a constant** — it scales every focus move, so it is bench-verifiable (command 100 µm, measure it) and correctable from the UI; likewise **focus direction is a checkbox** (`focus_up_is_positive`), because hard-coded axis polarity has bitten this codebase repeatedly (`ZDIR`, `z_up_sign`, `plate_flip_180`). `MicroscopeController` serializes **every** backend call onto **ONE dedicated worker thread** for two independent reasons: **COM apartment affinity** (an object must be used on its creating thread — same concern as the ToupCam/Andor backends) and **never blocking the Qt event loop** (a turret rotation is multi-hundred-ms; the freeze class already fixed three times by `PUMP_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD`/`STAGE_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD`/`JOG_TRAVEL_OFF_GUI_THREAD`). The widget **polls an immutable `MicroscopeState` snapshot** rather than registering a cross-thread callback — removing the listener-outlives-the-widget hazard (a callback firing into a deleted Qt object) entirely, at ≤400 ms render latency; two ordering details that would otherwise stick the UI: `busy=True` is published **while holding the queue lock** (else the worker's `busy=False` lands first and the panel shows a move that already finished) and ops older than `STALE_OP_S`=20 s are **dropped, not executed** (a turret move clicked 30 s ago is not what the operator wants now). NEW per-machine `SupportClasses/MicroscopeConfigStore.py` (`config/hardware/microscope.json`, atomic, `$MEBP_MICROSCOPE_CONFIG_DIR`, `get_store()`) owns the slot assignments — **NOT `HardwareConfig`**, which is the swappable print setup: a setup file from another machine must never carry another rig's cube assignments (the `CAMERA_CAL_PERSIST_STORE` lesson). Slots are **1-based everywhere** (matching the numbers engraved on the turret — the turret reports only a POSITION, so "slot 3 is mCherry" is knowledge only the operator has, which is the store's whole purpose); names beyond the slot count are hidden but never destroyed. NEW `gui/widgets/microscope_panel.py` (status + Connect + ⚙ · cube combo · objective combo · focus read-out · ▼/step/▲ · absolute go-to; polling stops while hidden, the shared connection is left alone) mounted as `Card("Microscope")` in `StandardJogContextPanel` (so it rides the **Jog pill everywhere that panel appears**) AND registered as a Custom-panel section — one widget, two surfaces, mirroring the illumination LED. Unblock with either the **full Nikon Ti SDK / NIS-Elements** (registers the COM object) or **Micro-Manager + `pip install pymmcore`**; the Code 28 driver must install first either way. NEW repo-root **`tools_microscope_hw_check.py`** (convention of `tools_render_geometry_panel.py`) runs the whole exercise through the REAL widget against any backend — prints `diagnostics()` **before** commanding anything, uses the operator's real saved config on hardware and never rewrites their slot assignments, and ⚠ **focus moves are OPT-IN (`--focus`)** because the focus drive moves the objective toward the specimen (blind move + loaded dish = objective into glass): read-only by default, else bounded by `--step` (5 µm) with the start position always restored. Verified end-to-end through the real widget+controller on the simulated backend (same code path, only the driver layer substituted): **26/26** tool checks + **35/35** scripted — assignment+restart persistence, per-slot cube switching with read-back, per-position objective switching with read-back, focus ±/absolute/direction-inversion/soft-limit clamp, impossible slot reported without moving the turret, disconnect disabling controls. **🐞 BUG FOUND BY THAT TESTING: the focus jog buttons SILENTLY DROPPED CLICKS** — `_render` disabled ▲/▼ whenever `state.busy`, so a click landing while the previous move was in flight hit a disabled button and vanished (two ▼ after one ▲ netted 0 µm, not −10). Repeated small steps are THE most common microscope interaction and queued focus deltas are additive + step-bounded, so dropping the click is strictly worse than queueing it: ▲/▼ now stay live during a move while go-to + both turret combos stay busy-gated (not additive; re-entrant selection mid-rotation is confusing). Also confirmed deliberate: a **background refresh does NOT clear a previous error** (it fires every ~1 s — clearing there would wipe the message before it could be read; the next successful command clears it). **⛔ MICRO-MANAGER ROUTE INVESTIGATED 2026-07-30 — DOES NOT UNBLOCK EITHER, so MM was deliberately NOT installed:** MM's own NikonTI docs are explicit — *"This adapter uses the driver and API supplied by Nikon"* — and require Nikon's **Ti Setup Tool** (registration-walled at Nikon Healthcare's SDK site), which installs `C:\Program Files\Nikon\Shared\Bin\NikonTi.dll` + the `Shared\Drivers` directory Windows must be pointed at to clear Code 28; **the MM adapter WRAPS Nikon's driver, it does not replace it**, and verification found NO `C:\Program Files\Nikon`, NO `NikonTi*.dll` anywhere on C:, no `Shared\Drivers` → the MM path is blocked by the SAME missing component, so a ≈200 MB system-wide install would have changed nothing (`pymmcore-plus` also declined — **40 packages** incl. botocore/s3fs/aiohttp/tensorstore onto a lab control machine). **`pymmcore` 12.5.0.75.0 WAS installed** (777 kB, numpy-only dep; **pinned `numpy==2.4.6` confirmed untouched** so the numba/Andor cap holds) + added to `requirements.txt` as optional — and having the real MMCore present bought **2 correctness wins**: (a) **all 11 `CMMCore` methods** the backend calls verified to exist with compatible arity (a typo would otherwise have surfaced mid-session at the bench), with empty/bogus-config paths confirmed raising readable `MicroscopeError`s and disconnected reads returning `None`/`0`/`()`; and this rig is a **Ti-E**, first-gen, not a Ti2, so the older 4.4.x kit is correct).** Downloaded (SHA256 `706500BA…8950`, 1.2 MB) and inspected **without installing** via `msiexec /a`: payload holds `Nikon\Shared\Bin\NikonTi.dll` **v4.4.1.714** (self-registering COM server per the MSI `SelfReg` table), `Nikon\Shared\Drivers\micusb.inf/.sys/.cat` (**the USB driver = the Code 28 fix**) and Nikon's own `ObjectiveNames.txt` (199) / `FilterBlockNames.txt` (34: DAPI, UV-2A, V-2A…) name tables. **🐞 THE PROGID WAS WRONG, corrected FROM THE DLL with no hardware**: `NikonTi.dll` publishes **61 `Nikon.TiScope.*` classes** — root **`Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi`** plus `Nosepiece`/`FilterBlockCassette1`/`ZDrive`/`XDrive`/`YDrive`/`PFS`/`PiezoZDrive`/… — and the string **`LvMic` occurs NOWHERE in the payload**, so `Nikon.LvMic.NikonTi` (taken from Micro-Manager-era notes) is **not** what a Ti/Ti-E SDK registers; `TI_PROG_IDS` now tries `Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi` FIRST with the LvMic entries kept only as other-generation fallbacks. **The device aliases were already RIGHT** (`Nosepiece`/`FilterBlockCassette1`/`ZDrive` ARE the SDK's own class names) so the probing layer needed no change. This resolves statically the exact unknown flagged "unverified" at design time. the script says so rather than failing silently. **⭐⭐ SDK INSTALLED — REAL-HARDWARE SESSION 2026-07-30:** elevated install succeeded (MSI exit 0, `NikonTi.dll` v4.4.1.714, `pnputil` **bound** the driver to `USB\VID_04B0&PID_7832` → device now correctly named **"Nikon USB Microscope"**), and **`Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi` connected**, exposing exactly the predicted tree (`Nosepiece`/`FilterBlockCassette1,2`/`ZDrive`/`XDrive`/`YDrive`/`PFS`…) — the ProgID correction is **confirmed on hardware**. **🔴 REMAINING BLOCKER IS HVCI, AND MY SHA-1 THEORY WAS WRONG:** the device moved Code 28 → **Code 39 `CM_PROB_DRIVER_FAILED_LOAD`**, and CodeIntegrity event **3111** names the real cause — *"attempted to load micusb.sys that is **not compatible with hypervisor enforcement**, Failure bitmap 0x2, Status 0xC0000220"* — corroborated by `Win32_DeviceGuard SecurityServicesRunning=2` (**Memory Integrity RUNNING**); a 2012 driver mapping W+X memory cannot load under HVCI. **Fix = Windows Security → Device security → Core isolation → Memory integrity → Off → reboot** (targeted; Secure Boot + signature enforcement stay ON). Downgrading to redist 4.4.1.672 would NOT help (older driver 2.0.2.1 vs 2.0.5.1). **Recorded as a trap: old-driver + Code 39 LOOKS like a signature problem, and the signature fix would not have worked.** **⭐ SDK OBJECT MODEL MAPPED, correcting two more things: NO device property returns a number** — every one returns an **`IMipParameter`** carrying `RawValue` (**the number**), `RangeLowerLimit/HigherLimit/Increment`, `Unit`, `DisplayString` (`IsMounted` → **"Device not available"**), `IsReadOnly`. Reading the *wrapper* is what produced the raw pointer bytes that crashed `int()`; dereferencing `RawValue` makes turret reads **WORK** (filter + nosepiece both `RawValue=1, range 1..6`). **🐞 A 100× FOCUS ERROR AVOIDED: the Z drive declares `Unit='um'` and range `0…400000` — it is µm-NATIVE, so the true scale is 1.0 units/µm, NOT the configured 100**; `focus_units_per_um()` now **READS the SDK's declared unit** (um/nm/mm) and only falls back to the configured number when none is declared (setting relabelled "fallback"), retiring the item flagged as *"the single most important check on the SDK path"* — it no longer needs bench measurement; `focus_limits_um()` likewise returns the declared 0–400000 µm travel. **Operator-readable errors:** a raw `COMError` used to dump a full traceback per failed op; `_com_message()` extracts the SDK's own words so the log reads **`No available instruments. [Nikon.TiScope.Nosepiece.1]`**. The two earlier tests encoding the *guessed* `Position.Value` model were **deleted, not patched** (they asserted an object model that does not exist), replaced by `_TiDevice`/`_MipParam` fakes modelled on the real interface. **✅ HARDWARE VERIFIED 2026-07-30 — 22/22 once Memory Integrity was disabled + rebooted** (`micusb.sys` loads → body live): connect via `Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi`; **all 6 filter slots** and **all 6 objective positions** switched with per-move read-back and start positions restored (objective started at 2, restored to 2); focus read **20045.0 µm** with the scale auto-resolved to **1.0 units/µm** and travel **0–400000 µm**. Focus **MOVES** remain the one unverified feature — deliberately opt-in (`--focus`) since the drive moves the objective toward the specimen. **🐞 SAFETY BUG FOUND BY THAT RUN — THE SDK SILENTLY CLAMPS: asked for filter slot 999 it moved the turret to slot 6 and reported SUCCESS** (no error, no exception) — for a *discrete* selector that is the worst failure mode, since a mistyped/stale/off-by-one slot would quietly image through the WRONG cube while the software called the move good; and it is precisely the gap a simulator cannot expose (`SimulatedMicroscopeBackend` had always *raised* for an out-of-range slot, so it looked correct until real hardware disagreed). NEW `_set_turret` validates against the SDK's **own declared** `RangeLowerLimit`/`RangeHigherLimit` and **refuses before writing** (test asserts the device is never written); **deliberately asymmetric — the focus drive CLAMPS to its declared travel instead**, because a continuous axis reaching end-of-travel during a jog is normal operation, not a mistake. **⭐ HARDWARE SETUP → MICROSCOPE TAB + "READ FROM MICROSCOPE"** (operator: *"lets make a hardware configuration tab for the microscope so I can setup the objectives and filter cubes"*). **The body knows its own optics** — probing the live SDK found `FilterBlockCassette1.FilterBlocks` (per slot `Name` = **DAPI/FITC/TxRed/Cy5**, `Code` 0 + name `-----` = empty) and `Nosepiece.Objectives` (per position `Name`/`ProductCode` **MRH20040**, `NumericalAperture`, `WorkingDistance`, and `Magnification` which is an **INDEX into the SDK's own table, not a magnification** — verified against `ObjectiveMagnifications.txt` AND cross-checked on hardware: codes 5/7/9 → **4x/10x/20x**, consistent with the NA/WD those objectives report; an empty position has `Code==0` and every other field RAISES *"No database code is associated with this optical element"*). NEW `MountedOptic` + `mounted_filters()`/`mounted_objectives()` on the backend contract (`()` = driver can't report), implemented for Nikon AND the simulator (page exercisable with no scope), surfaced on `MicroscopeState`, populated on connect + `refresh_mounted()` — deliberately **OFF the ~1 s poll** since optics only change when physically swapped. NEW `gui/pages/hardware/microscope_setup_panel.py` **`MicroscopeSetupPanel`** = the **ONE** setup surface: connection (driver + per-driver options + Connect + live status + Diagnostics), then Filter-cube and Objective tables of **# · what the body reports · your name · Go** with a slot-count spin and **↓ Read from microscope** filling the names from hardware (per-row **Go** rotates the turret so you can SEE what's in a slot; current slot outlined; Go/Read gated on a live connection), plus Focus (live position + declared travel, step, direction, soft limits); **nothing reaches the store until Save**. Registered as Hardware Setup sub-page **"Microscope"** (after Cameras); the Plate tab's icon moves `microscope`→**`grid`** (a plate IS a grid of wells, freeing the microscope icon for the microscope). **ANTI-DIVERGENCE: `MicroscopeSettingsDialog` was rewritten as a thin WRAPPER embedding the same panel** (OK=`commit()`, Cancel=discard) — two independently-written surfaces onto one set of settings is exactly the `UNIFIED_MOSAIC_CALIBRATION` failure. Also an unnamed slot in the jog card now falls back to **the body's own name** before "(empty)" (operator name wins, hardware name next). **Verified on the real Ti-E: one click filled `DAPI/FITC/TxRed/Cy5` + `4x/10x/20x`** (showing `MRH20040 · NA 0.13 · WD 16.4 mm` alongside), empty slots marked, focus `22,564.00 µm (travel 0–400,000 µm)`, Save round-tripped. **🐞 TWO OPERATOR-REPORTED BUGS 2026-07-30, both fixed. (1) FOCUS MOVES WERE 40× TOO SMALL** (*"trying to move up 1000 µm but it only moved 10 microns"*) — my earlier "auto-detect" read `ZDrive.Position.Unit=='um'` and concluded µm-native (factor 1.0), but **`Unit` describes the DISPLAY value, NOT `RawValue`**; the SDK's real model is `display [in Unit] = RawValue × DisplayScale`, and measured `DisplayScale=0.025` + `Unit='um'` ⇒ **0.025 µm (25 nm) per raw unit ⇒ 40 units/µm**. Confirmed 3 independent ways: `RawValue 30844`→`DisplayString '771.100 um'` (30844×0.025=771.100 exact), `ZDrive.Resolution=25` with the ZDrive's *device* `Unit='nm'`, and range `400000`→`'10000.000 um'` = **10 mm** (the Ti's real travel — **400 mm, what the old reading implied, should have been the tell**). `focus_units_per_um()` now computes `1/(DisplayScale × unit_to_um(Unit))` with the configured value used only when no `DisplayScale` exists; `focus_limits_um()` uses the same conversion (travel now 0–10000 µm, not 0–400000). **HW-verified: our reading `771.175 µm` == the SDK's own `DisplayString '771.175 um'` exactly; commanded ±10/±5 µm each moved ±10.000/±5.000 µm; zero net drift.** The two earlier tests asserting the Unit-alone model were **deleted, not patched** (they encoded the bug). **(2) THE BACKGROUND POLL CLOSED AN OPEN DROP-DOWN** (*"every time it reads it cancels the dropdown box I have opened"*) — the ~1 s refresh sets `busy=True`, `_render` then disabled the combo, and a disabled combo snaps its list shut under the cursor (`_rebuild_slot_combos` would have cleared it outright); `_tick()` now skips polling entirely while any popup is open and `_render()` never disables/re-indexes/rebuilds a combo whose popup is showing, while a CLOSED combo still tracks the hardware. Tests: `test_v75x_nikon_ti_microscope.py` (**94**); regression context-panel/illumination/responsive-context/jog-nav (80) + workflow-settings/common-print-settings/jog-pump-fill/axis-speed×2/xz-custom-z (122) green, plus a full `gui.app` import + threaded connect→state→shutdown smoke. **⭐ CONNECT MOVED TO THE `Connect Hardware` CARD 2026-07-31** (operator: *"move the microscope connect button to the connection area for xy stage, zp stage"*) — the body is hardware, so it opens where hardware opens: a **Microscope** row now sits with XY / Z+Pumps / Xbox in `control_panel._build_connect_group` with the same **Connect · Simulate · Disconnect** + status badge. **Connect** uses the driver SAVED on Hardware Setup → Microscope (this row never decides the driver); **Simulate** goes through `connect("simulated")`, a backend override that is **not written to the store**, so a simulator run can never silently rewrite the operator's real driver choice (pinned by test). The badge resolves **asynchronously** — `connect()` queues onto the COM-owning worker thread, so the click only shows "Connecting…" and the outcome lands on a later `on_status_update()` via `_sync_microscope_badge()`, whose in-flight signal is the controller's own `busy && !connected` (not a match on the badge's text). Two placement details follow from the microscope being its **own singleton**, not part of `StageController`: the sync runs **before** the `_controller is None` early-return (so the badge is live with no stage controller injected), and everything is `hasattr('badge_scope')`-guarded because the calibration variant builds this panel `show_connect=False` with no connect card at all. **REMOVED** from `MicroscopeSetupPanel`: its Connect button + `_toggle_connect`, which also **wrote `backend`+`prog_id` to the store on every click** so an unsaved driver choice was used — a hidden write now gone; the panel still REPORTS the link (its Go / ↓ Read need it) and states the consequence: **a driver change takes effect on the next connect after Save**. **NOT removed: the jog card's own Connect** — `StandardJogContextPanel` builds `HardwareControlPanel(show_connect=False)`, so the jog pill has no Connect Hardware card and taking its button away too would leave that surface unable to connect at all. Tests: `TestConnectHardwareRow` (6) → suite **101**; regression context-panel/jog-nav/device-page/pump-plunger/axis-speed + camera/hardware-setup **273** green. |
| `MEBP_v78_SPHEROID_DETECT_MEASURE_AND_PICK.md` | v7.8 **spheroid detection, measurement and curated picking** (operator: *"a toggle mode where each spheroid is detected and a circle is fit around the spheroid to measure its true radius … scan the entire well with a fluorescent mosaic … detect the location and size of all spheroids within a user defined range … can be modified by the user to redraw it for actual diameter … any user selected spheroids should have an image taken and cropped to the bounds … saved in a folder … training data (do not build this training system) … click on a list of spheroids … filtered by size ascending or descending … moves to that spheroid location and the user can click on it … goto, edit, or delete … a needle ID that is at least 1.5x the spheroid diameter"*). AskUserQuestion: **a second tab** with an explicit curated transfer into the pick list · the tab hosts a **live INSTANCE of the Fluorescence Mosaic page** (*"if we make changes to the fluorescent scan workflow it should automatically update this page like an instance"* — `embedded=True` drops only its back-button header, the `FullPrintWorkflowPage`/`PrintingModePage` re-home pattern, so there is exactly ONE single-well scan implementation and it cannot diverge) · per-spheroid volume **opt-in, default ON**, always × the safety factor, whose range widens **1.0–5.0 → 0.01–10** · redraw by **BOTH gestures** (drag the ring, or 3+ rim points) on **both** the mosaic and the live feed · crops from a **fresh live capture**. **⚠ THE DANGEROUS PART, FIXED FIRST: mosaic px → stage µm was not trustworthy.** `MosaicBuilder.canvas_extent_um` **ADDS** `_global_shift_um` to the extent while tile PIXELS stay at raw stage positions (hence `MosaicStore.save`'s `shift_um` + `calibration._ploc_mosaic_world_shift`), but the fluorescence worker called `finalize_global_shift()`, read the now-shifted extent, and saved it through a `save_channel` with **no `shift_um` parameter** — discarding it. `_max_shift_px()` at the default `max_shift_um=0` bounds that shift to **20 % of the FOV width ≈ ±337 µm at 10×, larger than a spheroid**; at `pick_z_offset_mm=0.10` that puts a pulled glass tip on the glass a quarter-millimetre off target. So: `save_channel(shift_um=)` + `get_shift_um` + **`has_shift`** (which distinguishes *recorded as zero* from **never recorded** — the actual motion gate; a legacy mosaic still displays and MEASURES fine because a diameter is translation-invariant, but **Go to and Transfer are disabled** with the error bound stated in µm) + `MEBP_FLUOR_MOSAIC_PATH`/`get_store(path=)`; the worker now captures the shift via `getattr(builder,"_global_shift_um",(0,0))` and `finished_ok` carries it. **A transferred position is a SEARCH HINT** — stamped `PROV_MOSAIC`, drawn **dashed**, listed `~mosaic`, upgraded to `PROV_CONFIRMED` only when a live click re-derives it through `pixel_to_stage_offset`; the operator's own "go there then click it" loop is therefore the safety mechanism, not a convenience. **NEW pure `SupportClasses/SpheroidDetector.py`** — a **SIBLING** of `WellDetector.detect_filled_wells`, never an edit of it: that function's gate is *relative* (`min_area_frac` of the LARGEST blob, floor 50 px², **no maximum**), right for "the N biggest discs on a plate mosaic" and catastrophic here because the biggest bright blob in one well is the **well wall/meniscus**, which both survives AND raises the floor above every real spheroid (a test pins that ours finds them and the legacy gate finds fewer) — and it feeds `MosaicWellRemap.detect_raw_positions`, whose well centres command motion. Reuses its PRIMITIVES via a new public `VisionDetector.fit_circle_robust` delegate. **TWO separately named shape gates**, because `detect_filled_wells`' `circularity_min` is actually a circle-fit **inlier fraction** whose 0.55 default is tuned to accept a *partial well arc*: `fit_fraction_min` 0.60 + genuine isoperimetric `circularity_min` 0.80 (the metric that rejects a doublet, whose min-enclosing-circle inlier fraction looks fine). **`THRESH_TRIANGLE` by default** — Otsu assumes bimodality and a ~95 %-background mosaic is unimodal, so Otsu picks a level INSIDE the background and floods the mask — plus a `bg_mean+3σ` floor, a morphology kernel scaled to the requested minimum (a fixed 7×7 stops meaning anything once the mosaic scale changes), canvas-margin masking (the extent carries half-FOV padding, so edge blobs are clipped arcs), and a default-on well-boundary mask. The gate is on the **FITTED** diameter (that is what becomes the measurement), area only a loose prefilter. `back_project_px` is **the one and only** px→stage-µm implementation (`extent[:2] − shift + px/scale`), pinned against `MosaicWellRemap` so the two cannot drift. **Detection may legitimately return ZERO** — on a nuclear stain a spheroid is a cluster of puncta, not a filled disc, so the realistic outcomes are "0 found" and "hundreds" — hence `DetectionReport`'s per-reason counters (*saw 14 blobs → 3 in band · 5 too small · 4 too large · 2 not round*), an actionable `refuse_reason` (*"an 80 µm spheroid is only 3 px across in this mosaic"*), and **manual add as a FIRST-CLASS path** (click the mosaic, or 3 rim points). Touching spheroids deliberately **not split** in v1 (a doublet exceeds `max_diameter_um` — literally the operator's rule — and watershed over-segments fluorescent spheroids, shipping confidently-wrong diameters). Detection runs on **ONE raw channel**, never `composite_overlay`'s blend (grayscale + saturating `cv2.add` clips overlapping channels and pushes a saturated edge outward → over-reads Ø), and the stored scale is **cross-checked against the image width** (>1 % ⇒ refuse) because `composite_overlay` resizes later channels to channel 0's shape and returns channel 0's extent without saying which; `composite_plate_overlay` (own derived scale) is never back-projected from. **NEW pure `SupportClasses/SpheroidTrainingStore.py`** — crops from the **RAW** `get_current_frame()` array, never a pixmap (the picker paints the crosshair and target rings ONTO its pixmap, so a display crop would bake our own annotation ring into every training image), `pad_frac` 0.25 (a zero-context bbox is untrainable; more pulls in a neighbour), clamped rather than **black-padded** (a synthetic border is a learned artefact), and two refusals that matter more than the happy path: a **clipped circle** (half a spheroid mislabels the Ø) and the **stage not being on the target** (a crop of somewhere else filed under this Ø is worse than no crop). `MEBP_SPHEROID_TRAINING_DIR`, `MAX_SAMPLES` 5000 oldest-first trim with unlink. **PER-SPHEROID VOLUME needed almost no code**: `PickPlaceTarget.size_um` has serialized since v7.3.3 and both overlay renderers already scaled from it, but **nothing ever wrote it**; `PickPlaceOperation.config` is already per-op and `_planned_aspirate_uL` reads only `compute_volume_uL()`, so one `dataclasses.replace` per pair in `_on_start` suffices with **NO executor change** — and an unmeasured pick keeps the **SAME config OBJECT**, which is what makes the queue byte-identical to pre-v7.8 (pinned by test). `size_um` is clamped 1–5000 µm on read now that it moves a volume scaling as **diameter cubed** (200→350 µm is **5.4×**). The **1.5× rule** needed none either: `NeedleSpec.spheroid_pickup_detail`'s `clearance` kwarg (never once overridden) computes `orifice_id_um / d_sph`, so `clearance=1.5` **IS** the rule — now a setting, surfaced **per row** (`⚠ tight 1.25×`), as an aggregate (*orifice 300 µm at 1.50× → up to 200 µm; 2 of 3 picks exceed it: P002, P003*) and at Start, evaluated **per pair** (one shared diameter would ignore the rest) and **never blocking**. Also warns when a fixed `release_volume_uL` meets varying aspirates (the residual then accumulates against the needle's internal volume). **🐞 REAL BUG FOUND EN ROUTE: `_PickerCameraView.to_px` was the plain identity inverse** while the click path applies mirror → flip_y → R(θ), so on any rotated/mirrored camera the marker did **not** land under the click — cosmetic for a point, a correctness bug the moment the operator resizes a circle or clicks a spheroid a Go-to just travelled to; fixed via a new `CameraManager.stage_offset_to_pixel` living **beside** the forward map so they cannot drift, and pinned by a click→stage→overlay→click **closure** test over `(rotation, flip_x, flip_y) ∈ {0,90,180,270,37.5}×{F,T}×{F,T}` — at θ=0 with no flips the buggy and correct forms AGREE, which is exactly why it survived (a companion test asserts the naive inverse is >50 px wrong at 90°). Every back-projection test uses a **non-zero** shift AND origin for the same reason. **Picker rework:** selection captured/restored **by target_id** around the full list rebuild (it died on every add/remove, making a row's own actions unusable), ids in `Qt.UserRole`, deletion **by id never row index**; `[Go to] [Edit…] [Delete]` per list, enabled on exactly one selection, Go-to **emitting** for the host (the picker commands no motion); **multi-select finally enabled** — "Remove selected" always intended plural (its loop walks rows in reverse) but the lists sat on the default single-selection mode; removal radius scaled by `size_um` (the flat 250 µm is narrower than a 400 µm spheroid's own ring). **Live-feed measure mode** (OFF = byte-identical legacy, the non-regression guarantee, so `pick_only`/Cell Labeling is untouched): **ring before centre** hit test in WIDGET px (`_SNAP_RADIUS_WIDGET_PX=12`; a frame-px tolerance is 3 px at 4K and 40 at VGA) so resizing a 40 µm circle is never hijacked into a move, drag clamped to the frame, and the µm↔px **projection FROZEN at press** — the host polls the stage at 5 Hz, so a drag spanning a settle would silently resize the circle. Editable **mosaic** circles reuse the two proven precedents deliberately: rect in ABSOLUTE scene coords with `pos()` left at (0,0) so centre = `rect().center()+pos()` (the `_place_marker`/`_marker_center` convention whose arithmetic already drives the stage), a view-driven radius handle à la `RadiusHandleItem` (**not** `ItemIsMovable`), and label+handle as **CHILDREN** so a drag carries them — `mosaic_well_mapping_dialog` makes them siblings and its labels lag, an existing bug deliberately not retrofitted. **No in-scene numeric widgets** (`plate_designer_canvas` records that proxy spin boxes crashed). `_ZoomImageView` gains opt-in `set_interactive_items` (left button to items, middle-button pan; **default off** so the Fluorescence page is unchanged). **One `_stage_busy()`** replaces `_travel_blocked_by_run` across **all four** motion entry points and now covers the scan worker, which drives `safe_travel_to` and toggles the **NON-refcounted** `suspend_position_poller` (whichever finished first would re-enable it under the other); Start disabled during a scan and vice-versa. **Absolute vs zero-ref µm kept in SEPARATE handlers** (`_travel_to_absolute` vs `_on_workspace_position_clicked`) — sharing one would add `zero_position` twice, a millimetres-off move; a test asserts the same numbers reach DIFFERENT destinations, that `SafeTravelWorker` gets `target_z_mm=None` (retract, wait, travel, never descend), and that no bare `move_xy_absolute*` is ever called. Detection + crop capture are both threaded (a 12 000 px mosaic is seconds of OpenCV; `capture_fresh_frame` is forbidden from a worker by its own docstring, so the `frame_count_value()` pattern is used). Detection params + mode flags persist via `set_extra_state`; the **detected list deliberately does NOT** (a restored coordinate from another plate is a crash, and nothing can prove the mosaic is still the one loaded). **⚠ EXISTING INSTALLS MUST RE-SCAN a well before picking from it** — pre-v7.8 mosaics have no `shift_um` and theirs is unrecoverable. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER** (1 = go/no-go: scan → Go to → residual within µm/px noise for **three spheroids near the mosaic CORNERS**, since a *scale* error grows with distance and is invisible at one central point; then five-point axis/sign sanity — a mirrored axis reads as "right distance, wrong way"; Ø cross-checked against `MeasurementCameraView`; both redraw gestures agreeing; the advisory firing **while Start still runs**; two visibly different spheroids logging two different aspirate volumes; crops landing **un-annotated**). Tests: `test_v78_spheroid_detection.py` (39) + `test_v78_fluor_mosaic_shift.py` (15) + `test_v78_spheroid_training_store.py` (37) + `test_v78_spheroid_per_target_volume.py` (43) + `test_v78_target_actions.py` (48) + `test_v78_spheroid_survey_tab.py` (49) + `test_v78_spheroid_page_integration.py` (50) = **281 green**; regression fluorescence-mosaic/spheroid×3/cell×2/camera-store/camera-rotation/mosaic-orientation-remap/workflow-settings green, `plate_mosaic` 110 with its two documented pre-existing problems unchanged. ⚠ **Pre-existing environmental flake, proven not mine:** running the camera-probing suites together in ONE process intermittently hangs on real DirectShow enumeration — the identical 9-suite command timed out in a pristine `git worktree` at HEAD as well (exit 124), and completed with exit 0 at both HEAD and this branch on another attempt; every suite passes alone. |
| `MEBP_v75x_UNIFIED_MOSAIC_CALIBRATION.md` | v7.5.x **ONE mosaic/camera calibration surface** (operator: *"we have multiple surfaces for calibrating mosaics, there should only be one and it should be in the camera calibration section … after settings are applied, there should be a final check with the new settings for confirmation … we also need the ability to rotate the entire mosaic to ensure the output is the correct way up and down … Rosette, full plate overview, and fluorescence workflow should all have the exact same behavior. Currently everything is very messed up."*). Investigation found **14 surfaces** writing overlapping camera/mosaic calibration and **6 mosaic-build pipelines**, each resolving its inputs with its own precedence — the features existed, the PLUMBING had diverged.
