AskUserQuestion: one surface in **Hardware Setup → Cameras** · duplicates **removed entirely** · confirmation = a **small test mosaic** · **commit gated on Accept** · overlap **default 25 %, adjustable** · whole-mosaic rotation **auto + a persisted display-only override** · **needle cams left alone** so `TwoCameraNeedleAligner`'s `column_dir_deg` contract cannot regress. Mapped to the operator's five deviation types: **(1) mirror (2) stage ±X/±Y vs ±pixel axes (3) rotation about +Z** are ONE 2×2 camera→stage matrix, already measured together by `derive_camera_stage_orientation` (`scale_fov_calibration_dialog.py:77`) and decomposed into the canonical `(rotation_deg, flip_x, flip_y)` that `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile` / `CameraFeedView` / `pixel_to_stage_offset` all consume — but **P5 (fluorescence) consumed NONE of it**, applying the retired coarse `mosaic_scan.frame_orient` string instead, so on this machine's camera (stored `rotation 180 + flip_y`) it rotated the tiles but **LOST THE FLIP** while the plate scan applied both: same camera, two orientations. **(4) pixel size**: `CameraCalibrationStore` had **no resolution stamp for `um_per_px`** and `_restore_calibration_for_slot` called `set_um_per_px()` without `resolution=`, so after every restart `effective_um_per_px` degraded to a **passthrough** — and this machine's Andor is calibrated at TWO resolutions (4× 3.227 @1024, 10× 1.320 @2048), a 2× error. **(5) overlap** resolved to 5 % (live) / 25 % (P4) / 0.50 (P6) / a dead `_PLOC_MOSAIC_OVERLAP` depending on path; at 5 % a small µm/px error opens GAPS (spacing = FOV × (1 − overlap)) and starves registration of texture, so (4) and (5) compounded. Two hidden top-precedence overrides made it worse: `mosaic_scan.fov_um` (an Advanced-submenu spin beating every measured value, forever) and the learned `MosaicAlignmentStore` "Store FOV/spacing" value **shadowing the objective calibration just measured** ("I literally just calibrated it"). **NEW pure `SupportClasses/MosaicCalibration.py`** — frozen `MosaicCalibration` + `resolve()` (ONE precedence: objective calibration → live manager → refuse; camera store → manager → neutral for orientation) + `build_mosaic_builder()` (the single place builder kwargs are chosen — what makes the pipelines identical *by construction*; they previously disagreed on `target_mosaic_px` 3000/1800/2500/2000, `registration_method`, and whether `retain_for_reorient` was set at all, and **without it the caller's `optimize_registration` silently did nothing**, so the operator tuned against a mosaic stitched more weakly than the scan he then ran) + `refuse_reason()` + `warnings_for()` + per-field **provenance**. Store **v1.1→1.2** adds `um_per_px_resolution` with a backfill from a uniquely-matching objective entry, else `hw_controls.resolution`, else **left absent — absent means UNKNOWN, never assumed** (verified on a copy of the real `camera_calibrations.json`: Andor recovered `3.227061 @ 1024×1024`, ToupCam likewise, six Teslongs from `hw_controls`, one correctly unstamped, re-load byte-identical). NEW `CameraManager.restore_calibration_from_store()` called from `start`/`start_async` so orientation is live **regardless of page-visit order** (previously only the Hardware Setup page pushed it — why P3/P4 built un-oriented). **The one surface**: the card becomes **"Mosaic & Camera Calibration"** with one button; NEW `gui/dialogs/mosaic_calibration_confirm_dialog.py` states the measurement in operator language (`_axis_phrase`: *"Moving the stage +X moves the image left; +Y moves it down"*), hosts overlap (**priced in tiles**) + output rotation, and offers **Build test mosaic…** through the shared path — with **nothing persisted until Accept**, which required fixing two dialogs that wrote `set_mirrored` on the checkbox's **first click, even on Cancel**. **Whole-mosaic output rotation is DISPLAY-ONLY** (composed into `JogWorkspaceView`'s existing render cache + `MosaicRegistrationView`); baking it into the composite/extent would break the `stage = (extent − shift) + px/scale` back-projection every consumer uses to derive well centres for MOTION — the class of bug that once drove the stage millimetres off target — and a test pins that it never reaches `MosaicBuilder`. **Removed:** `frame_orient` + `fov_um`/`spacing_um` controls, the `fov_um` spin and its "Apply to full mosaic" write-back, `ObjectiveCalibrationDialog` (verified zero instantiations), the legacy per-well double-click scan (hardcoded 3.34 µm/px / 0.50 overlap / desyncing `build_mosaic`), `_PLOC_MOSAIC_OVERLAP`. **Live views:** `auto_orient=True` on 8 microscope feeds incl. the rosette one (**highest priority — its clicks become sub-well centres**) + `MeasurementCameraView` now forwards the kwarg; needle feeds stay raw. **3 bugs found beyond the plan:** `live_target_picker` pushed the manager's own µm/px back with `resolution=None`, **clearing a correct stamp**; `set_calibrated_um_per_px` shared one `try` across three pushes so a µm/px failure also skipped `column_dir_deg` (**the value the needle aligner refuses to run without**); and `test_frame_orient_transforms_tile` had been **failing unnoticed** behind a pre-existing `TestManualAlignPage` hang while contradicting its own sibling. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (run the one calibration → measured mirror/rotation match the bench; test mosaic stitches with no gaps and the output rotation puts it the right way up; Cancel leaves the previous calibration untouched; switch 4×→10× → FOV changes ~2× automatically **and survives a restart** — the regression that used to break silently; all three mosaics oriented+scaled **identically** (fluorescence previously lost the flip_y); map wells and drive to two — the needle must land on the centres, proving the display-only rotation didn't disturb back-projection). Tests: `test_v75x_unified_mosaic_calibration.py` (**37**) + regression 380 green + `plate_mosaic` 104 (2 pre-existing problems unchanged: the documented `test_real_24_well_mosaic` CV failure and the `TestManualAlignPage` hang — **that hang makes a whole-suite run never terminate and hides real failures**). Remaining by choice: the shared-worker extraction (behaviour already unified), the Custom-tab Camera Setup legacy panel, and the `micron_per_pixel_override` UI. |
| `MEBP_v75x_STARTUP_WELL_MAP_MOSAIC_OFFSET.md` | v7.5.x **fix — "every time I start the software, the wells are not mapped correctly to the mosaic for the full plate; I have to press Map wells each time"** (operator, 2026-07-29). Root cause, verified against the operator's own settings.json: the saved `plate_warp` stores (predicted→measured) pairs and `_load_calibration` re-applies the warp to a RECOMPUTED predicted grid — a contract that silently requires the fit-time and load-time prediction frames to match. They didn't: `set_hardware_config` re-seeded `_predicted_positions` with the geometry-only **envelope-centred** grid on EVERY config push (even with a live calibration), so the Map-wells warp was fitted against the envelope frame, while the restart rebuilt an **A1-anchored** frame (`_compute_predicted_positions` from the measured `taught_a1`) and evaluated the warp there → every well shifted by the A1 prediction error — on this machine warp src A1 (104993.5, 66167.35) = exactly the envelope grid vs taught_a1 (105617.6, 65890.4) ⇒ **~(+624, −277) µm ≈ 0.68 mm off the mosaic at every launch**; re-mapping re-fitted against the envelope frame again, so the cycle never converged. **Fixes (both in `gui/pages/calibration.py`):** (1) `set_hardware_config` gates the envelope seed on "nothing calibrated" (the `recenter_default_plate` gate) and otherwise rebuilds predictions FROM the taught A1 — one frame everywhere (also stabilises `_ploc_apply_global_translation`'s reload contract); calibrated-without-A1 leaves the grid untouched. (2) `_save_calibration` persists the explicit `calibrated_positions` **also when a warp exists** (previously only the no-warp re-derive wrote it) and `_load_calibration` restores them as the authoritative positions while STILL restoring the warp object (re-anchor folds corrections into it), reconstructing positions from the warp only for legacy saves — the restart is byte-identical to what the operator saw at save time. Deliberately NOT auto-applying the mosaic-stored `wells_um` at startup (would clobber a newer manual teach; no timestamps to arbitrate). **Existing installs: run Map wells… once after updating** (the on-disk warp is envelope-framed with no explicit dict); every restart after that reproduces the mapping. Tests: `tests/test_v75x_startup_well_map_persistence.py` (6, incl. the operator's exact frame-shift reproduced: legacy reconstruction >100 µm off while the restored calibration lands on the measured mapping); orientation-remap/freeform-warp/last-known-cal/predict-well/plate-centering/plate-types/orientation/reanchor/plate-mosaic/rosette-reanchor/single-well-reregister regression green. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (Map wells once → restart ×2 → rings sit on the mosaic wells with no re-map; re-anchor then restart keeps the shifted map). |
| `MEBP_v75x_XZ_CUSTOM_Z_LOCATIONS.md` | v7.5.x **custom Z locations on the Jog XZ side view** (operator: drag on the Z-axis view → live mm readout tag → release sets a custom location; clicking the current-Z line tags the needle's current height). Opt-in at the widget level — `XZSideView.set_custom_z_enabled(True)` is called ONLY by the Jog page, so the other four hosts (calibration Needle-Offset/Plate-Location tabs, spheroid/cell workflows) keep byte-identical mouse behaviour. Values stored in the RAW zero-ref mm frame (same as `_z_refs` + the `go_to_z_requested` emit → move contract/soft limits hold on both Z polarities); all display through the existing `_disp()` sign. Gesture: press inside the plot = pending; > ~4 px movement = live drag (dashed yellow ghost line + `Z +x.xxx mm — release to tag` pill, inverse mapping `_px_to_z_disp` through the ZOOMED bounds so dragging while zoomed is exact); plain click adds the current Z only within ~6 px of the current-Z line (stray plot clicks do nothing). Custom locations render in the same pass as the Z-ref badges (shared collision-nudge/leader) as `★ +x.xx mm` pills (click → `go_to_z_requested`, right-click removes) + an ✕ circle (removes). API: `set/custom_z_locations` (programmatic set does NOT emit — no save loop), `add/remove_custom_z` (dedup/match tol 0.005 mm), signal `custom_z_changed(list)`. Jog page persists per machine in settings.json `jog.custom_z_mm` (restore in `set_settings`, save on every change). **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_xz_custom_z_locations.py` (25: drag/readout/sign-frame/zoom, click-line vs click-elsewhere, badges/✕/right-click, dedup/no-emit-restore, off-by-default, Jog enable+persist+restore); XZ-side-view/jog regression 66 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_ZYLA_DISPLAY_AUTOSCALE_CONTROL.md` | v7.5.x **Zyla "camera keeps adjusting itself" → controllable display scaling** (operator: wants an auto-exposure/gain-style option for the Zyla + manual control). Root cause: the Zyla has NO ISP auto-gain — `AndorBackend._mono_to_bgr8` normalized EVERY frame to its own 1–99 percentile (mono16→BGR8 display conversion), so display brightness chased the scene. Now an option: backend display-scale state (`get/set_display_auto_scale`, `put_display_black/white` in raw counts 0..65535, `hi>lo` clamped; toggling auto OFF **freezes the last auto frame's levels** so the image keeps its current look) picked up by the reader loop; camera settings dialog gains **"Auto display scaling (per-frame)"** checkbox + **Black/White level** sliders (visible only on an Andor slot, sliders disabled while auto on; Defaults restores auto); deliberately NOT named "auto-exposure" (the real hardware exposure row stays independent — reusing the auto-exposure checkbox would wrongly disable it). Persisted per identity in `hw_controls` (`andor_auto_scale/_scale_lo/_scale_hi`), restored on camera start (auto flag FIRST, then levels, so stored levels beat the freeze-seed). Caps advertise `andor_*` controls; manager/widget `set_hw_andor_*` delegates return False on other backends. **Needs real-HW verification on the Zyla rig.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_andor_display_scaling.py` (21) + `FakeAndorCam` mirrored; andor/camera-controls/store/correction/liveview/rotation/async suites 176 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_NEEDLE_CAM_SYMMETRIC_45_AND_VIEW_ROLL.md` | v7.5.x **needle side cameras physically re-mounted symmetric about stage +X at +45°/−45° — mount direction split from display roll + manual needle center** (operator: cameras now aligned to +X at ±45; the ±45° stored as `rotation_deg` was tilting the live view; "Set current as location" didn't wire into the needle center). ONE conflated per-camera `rotation_deg` previously served BOTH the aligner's column→stage mount direction AND the display orientation. Split: NEW per-identity **`column_dir_deg`** (mount direction, consumed ONLY by `TwoCameraNeedleAligner` via `_needle_loc_camera_info`; `CameraCalibrationStore.get/set_column_dir` + `set_calibration(column_dir_deg=)`, `CameraManager.get/set_column_dir_deg`) while **`rotation_deg` becomes strictly the DISPLAY roll** — for needle cams the *deviation of the measured stage-motion vector from parallel*, computed by NEW `pixel_calibration_dialog.fold_parallel_deg`/`view_roll_from_displacement` → `result_view_roll_deg` (sign PINNED by a test composing it with the extracted pure `camera_feed_view.view_transform_coeffs` — the measured vector must display level for every angle × mirror × flip-Y). Commit paths split by role (needle card passes `rotation_deg=roll, column_dir_deg=mount`; `_on_calibrate_slot_rotation` needle branch + NEW `_apply_slot_column_dir`; restore pushes both; needle slot readout `mount −44.6° (Δ+0.4°) · roll +1.2°`). **The aligner REFUSES when either needle cam's direction is unmeasured** (operator: cams are interchangeable "Needle cam 1/2", which-is-which comes from the µm/px calibration) — the legacy orthogonal 90°/0° fallback would drive the stage the wrong way on this rig; roles relabelled "Needle cam 1/2" (`needle_role_label`; enum values `needle_x/needle_y` frozen — serialized). **Store migration v1.0→1.1**: a needle-assigned legacy `rotation_deg` MOVES to `column_dir_deg` (that WAS its semantic) so old machines' views render level immediately; fresh stores are born at 1.1 (else a first-write roll would be wrongly migrated — caught by test). **Manual needle center**: "Set current as needle center" now records the FULL center via factored `_needle_loc_record_origin_here()` (shared with Center & Save): `needle_origin_um` + quick-move XY + needle-cam Z fiducial, behind a confirm dialog. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (calibrate both needle cams → views level, mount ≈ +45/−45 measured; Center & Save lands the needle on both crosshairs; manual button records origin+XY+cam-Z; restart restores both fields). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_needle_cam_mount_and_roll.py` (31) + updated quick-move/store/rotation-monitor suites; ~420 regression green. |
| `MEBP_v77_QUICKPRINT_UX_AND_REPORTING.md` | v7.7 **Quick Print usability, live information, and the first post-print report** (operator: *"make it the best most straightforward as well as keep the user as informed as possible with all of the data we can give them. we may want to include post print reports to help the user understand the print output."*). AskUserQuestion: in-app Report view **+** self-contained HTML/CSV export reusing the four existing `print_results.py` QPainter widgets (**matplotlib stays out of the GUI**) · motion mode **auto-selects** per machine · bioprinting checks **advisory, hard-block only the physically impossible** · **three zones Setup/Run/Report**. **Why:** the v7.6 HW run proved the engine but showed the UI hid what the software knew — the DEFAULT `open_loop` mode made every prediction/overlay/time-estimate dark (`_kick_prediction` returns early unless `velocity`), the two knobs the code itself calls *"THE TWO PARAMETERS everything else derives from"* sat in a popout behind four inline controls, the simulator's 7 µm vs the machine's 27 µm (**3.75×**) had no feedback loop, and there was **no post-print report at all** — the JSONL exec log had **no reader anywhere in the repo**. **(0) 14 DEFECTS FIXED:** doubled progress counter (`[14/57] [14/57]` — `PrintManager._report_progress` already prefixes); terminal states now report WHY (the specific error / ZP-disconnect text was overwritten by a generic "Error — see log"); an abort/error states the fluidic consequences that lived **only in the log file** (cleanup did NOT run so the needle still holds ink; dispensed volume INDETERMINATE); **`Abort` live during ANY positioning** (was gated on an active executor → DEAD through a plain-print preposition while the stage was travelling); `Pause`/`Resume` exposed (existed, unreachable); `Open log`; dead `_update_top_speed_cap` finally called; the summary's silent `except: pass` now logs+falls back; post-clean refusal named a non-existent control; navigator tooltip said "fast-travel" where a click only selects; the nine silently-editing popout fields fixed **structurally** via `notify_on_field_change` (debounced, so no FUTURE field can go stale either); inline help falls back to each widget's tooltip + registers with the Help toggle (`help=` was passed on **zero** of ~25 fields); multi-ink mapping persists via a new generic `set_extra_state`; the prediction stays visible mid-print (`dev 38 µm (pred 7)`). **(1) NEW pure `SupportClasses/PrintReadiness.py`** (`Check`/`Readiness`/`ReadinessContext`/`evaluate`, zero Qt — 19 checks / 8 groups) replaces a single run-on wrapping label of up to ten `"   "`-joined clauses AND the four ANDed booleans behind Print, so a disabled Print NAMES every missing precondition. Two invariants pinned by tests: **unknown data never blocks** (a `None` getter must not disable Print forever) and **`can_print()` blocks on exactly the pre-existing gates** (XY+ZP, object, plate, well) **plus the one impossible case** (particles ≥ bore) — it cannot start refusing prints that used to run. Newly surfaced: which of the three limits binds **and the headroom when none does** (silent before unless exceeded), calibration staleness (`CalibrationStatusStore`, never read here), `StageCharacteristics.missing()`+`measured_at`, dead-time provenance (`measured`/`phase_lag`/`unmeasured` — whose docstring says it exists "so the UI can say WHICH number is capping the speed"), resolved ABSOLUTE print/travel Z **including when a travel height was synthesised**, taught-vs-geometric well centre, **object-fits-the-well** (a check that did not exist), bead width, path length + strokes, pickup breakdown, syringe fill. Advisories that never ran for a print: `InkSpec.flow_compatibility_detail` clog risk, `FlowPhysics.wall_shear_stress` vs the 5 Pa cell-viability limit, and the enforced ceiling disclosed as a **reference-fluid** figure. **(2) THREE ZONES** — `QStackedWidget` + pill strip; the two parameters **promoted onto Setup** yet still saved with the profile via new `WorkflowSettingsDialog.register_external` (a widget has one parent, so it cannot also live in a section); well selector moved to Setup; Run is the watch surface; Print→Run and terminal→Report auto-advance. **The attribute-name contract is pinned by a test** — the partial-page suites never build the UI, so a rename breaks them SILENTLY. **(3) LIVE NUMBERS:** `vel_sample` gains `v_meas_mm_s` (already computed and thrown away) + `deposited_uL`, plus `PrintManager.on_vel_sample` — all on the **existing every-5th-tick branch**, so the ~25 Hz control loop gains NO per-tick work; the Qt slot only STORES and rendering runs on the existing 10 Hz timer, so a slow paint can't back-pressure the loop. Shows progress+ETA (finally calling the public `progress_fraction()`), **live deviation vs the operator's OWN element** with a verdict + running max/p95 (both numbers were in hand, never compared), dispensed-vs-planned µL, section n/N, measured-vs-commanded speed. **(4) THE REPORT:** NEW pure `SupportClasses/PrintLogReader.py` — the **first reader** for `logs/prints/*.jsonl` (streaming, tolerates a truncated final line and a log still being written, counts `n_bad_lines`, never raises) + `to_recorder_samples` producing the exact `list[dict]` the four widgets consume **in mm** (verified: `_error_to_color` and `StatisticsPanel` both ×1000) + `section_stats`/`restart_decomposition`/`volume_reconciliation`/`problems`/`comm_health`; NEW `gui/pages/workflows/quick_print_report.py` — verdict badge, **predicted vs actual with the ratio**, the four reused widgets (path coloured by error, playback scrubber, error-vs-time, statistics), per-section table annotated with the plan's own `reason` AND the p95 measured inside it, the restart-vs-mid-section decomposition, fluidics reconciliation, machine health, problems + traceback; self-contained HTML (base64 PNGs via `QWidget.grab()`, no external URLs) + CSV. `PrintExecutionLogger.path_points()` now stores the (decimated, endpoint-preserving) PRINT_PATH points so a SAVED log can be scored — `manifest_for_job` recorded only `n_points`; older logs open with no deviation scoring and SAY so. `planned_x/y` = the ideal point at the achieved arc length, NOT the pursuit carrot (which sits a lookahead ahead, so error against it would measure the lookahead); `sample.lag_um` is deliberately never presented as tracking error (in velocity mode nothing updates its target inside the loop, so it degrades to "distance from the path start"). **⭐ VALIDATED AGAINST THE REAL v7.6 HARDWARE LOG** (`print_20260729_121416_*.jsonl`): 0 bad lines; section offsets reconstructed 0→25.78 mm; `restart_decomposition` returns **near-boundary p95 31.5 µm vs mid-section 19.8 µm**, independently reproducing the 31.7/17.8 computed BY HAND in the v7.6 session; the report scores it **p95 27.5 / rms 11.7 / max 35.9 µm PASS** with a predicted/actual ratio of **3.76** against the 3.75 computed by hand; planned volume 3.8373 µL = the harness's own figure; ZP 477/477 acked; time estimate 21.2 s vs 21.7 s (+2 %). **🐞 THREE BUGS FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, none in new code:** (a) `_reserve_warning` read `self._hw_config` with no getattr guard, breaking the `__new__`-partial pattern the whole suite relies on; (b) **`_needle_cross_section_mm2` was a class-level ALIAS of `_needle_orifice_area_mm2`** — the same function object, so overriding one name (as every partial-page test does) left the other pointing at the real implementation and **the flow-ceiling warning, which read the other name, had quietly stopped firing**; now a delegating method, and `_append_limit_warnings` uses the same accessor as its three siblings so the warning threshold and the commanded flow can never disagree about which area the bead uses; (c) **my own reader indexed the full-path arc length with a SECTION-LOCAL `s_mm`** (a feed-plan `vel_sample`'s `tot_mm` is only its section's length), which would have mis-projected every section after the first — fixed by reconstructing global arc length from each `plan_section`'s `base_s_mm` (works on logs already on disk); the same mistake was caught twice more in the live panel (progress % and planned volume from `tot_mm`) and corrected. **(5) CLOSED-LOOP DEFAULT + NEVER PAUSE ON CORNERS** (operator, superseding the auto-select design: *"the closed loop velocity mode should now be default. We should never pause on corners, just slow down. We always want the pump moving unless there is a break in the section that is planned."*): **closed-loop velocity is now the default motion mode** (open-loop reads NO position during the path, so every v7.7 information surface was inert in the old default); NEW **`XYFeedPlan.build_continuous_plan`** = ONE section with **arc-length profiles** for lookahead and speed instead of per-section scalars, wired through new optional `lookahead_at=` on `VelocityControl.pursuit_step` and `lookahead_at=`/`speed_limit_at=` on `XYPathSimulator.follow_path` (**both default None → the shared follower is byte-identical when unused**; 126 follower/plan tests unchanged); `PrintSettings.feed_plan_corner_policy` with **`"slow"` the default** and `"stop"` retaining the hardware-validated v7.6 sectioned plan, logged in `path_start` as `corner_policy`+`continuous`. The pump therefore never pauses mid-path (no split ⇒ no dwell, deposition tracks arc length), only at a genuinely planned pen-up — pinned by a monotone-deposition test. **🐞 TWO MODELLING BUGS THE SIMULATION CAUGHT — the first attempt was far WORSE, not better:** (a) **the arc formula does not describe a corner** — sizing the carrot with `la=0.8·√(2·R·δ)` at a sharp vertex asked 0.092 mm at 90° and simulated **40 µm** against an 18.75 µm budget, because on a polyline vertex the cut is `≈(la/2)·sin(θ/2)`, **LINEAR** in lookahead not quadratic; new `corner_budget_lookahead_mm(θ,δ)=2δ/sin(θ/2)` asks 0.053 mm and the plan takes the **min of both models** per vertex → Square **58.6→7.6 µm**, Star 54.9→7.5, Zigzag 62.6→7.7, Comb 58.0→7.6; (b) **gating knots on turn angle ignored smooth curvature** — a 5 mm circle sampled at 0.5 mm turns only ~11°/vertex, under any sane threshold, so circles ran at full lookahead and simulated **48.7 µm**; knots are now created wherever EITHER model binds, with no angle gate → Circle 5 mm **48.7→5.3 µm**, Circle 10 mm 25.4→3.1. Plus **lead compensation** (`LEAD_LAG_MARGIN`): the slowdown is commanded `v·(dead+τ+loop/2)·1.25` BEFORE the corner, approach-side only, for the same reason the v7.6 end taper had to be lag-aware. **⚠ ONE THING "never pause" CANNOT DO: a near-180° reversal still stops** (`REVERSAL_STOP_DEG=150°`) — a cusp cannot be traversed by pure pursuit at any speed, since a carrot placed forward in ARC LENGTH sits BEHIND the stage in SPACE; simulated without a stop, Line-Reversal ran to the wall cap (855 s) with no progress. Geometry, not policy, and the plan says so in its notes. **MEASURED TRADE (simulation, ME3B V1, 3 mm/s, 30 µm element): never-stopping holds the element just as well — 10/10 shapes pass, within ~1 µm of the stopping plan (Square 6.9→7.6, Star 5 8.1→7.5, Circle 10 3.1→3.1, Comb 8.0→7.6) — but costs ~1.5–1.9× the WALL TIME on corner-heavy geometry** (Star 10 mm 18.3→34.0 s, Zigzag 19.9→36.4, Comb 27.6→42.0), because crawling a corner at ~0.3 mm/s over a ~1 mm ramp costs more than a 1.3 s stop; on smooth shapes it is slightly faster. **The reason to prefer it is print quality — deposition is never interrupted — not speed.** Stage 5 is **simulation-verified only**; `feed_plan_corner_policy="stop"` restores v7.6 behaviour with no code change if the corner slowdown misbehaves on hardware. Still outstanding from v7.6: `PREDICTION_HEADROOM` is 1.6 in `XYFeedPlan` and 2.0 in `sketch_printability_dialog` and remains unreconciled — the report now measures the ratio every run so that call can be evidence-based. Tests: `test_v77_print_readiness.py` (39) + `test_v77_print_log_reader.py` (23, incl. truncated-file tolerance, `tracking_error_xy×1000 == cross_um`, the section-offset projection, and integration against the real log) + `test_v77_quick_print_zones.py` (54, incl. the attribute-name contract, the doubled-counter regression, HTML self-containment, the live panel); regression **496 green**. Two legitimate test updates: the new `predicted_p95_um` kwarg, and `test_v75x_print_setup_routine` still driving the `speed_pct` knob v7.6 retired. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (live panel through a velocity print with the camera running; the Report zone opening on the terminal state with its ratio matching a hand check; HTML/CSV opening outside the app). |
| `MEBP_v76_PULLED_GLASS_CAPILLARY_NEEDLE.md` | v7.6 **pulled glass capillary needles — two-stage needle geometry** (operator: *"on the needle hardware setup page we need the option of pulled glass capillary needles … an inner diameter and length for the bulk needle then a tip diameter and length where we pull the tip to a certain diameter"*, used differently by **1)** max flow rate, **2)** print line bead diameter, **3)** spheroid size we can pick up, **4)** needle volume for ink pickup). `NeedleSpec` modelled ONE straight cylinder (gauge from `needles.json` + a length combo) and ~30 call sites read that assumption; a capillary is a bulk barrel (D1,L1) feeding a pulled tip (D2,L2) with D2 typically 10–100 µm against a ~1 mm barrel, so all four consumers were wrong for it. **AskUserQuestion:** tip shape = **both profiles selectable** (`tip_profile` ∈ cylinder/cone, cylinder default) · needle volume = **total for prep multiples, tip-only for the Quick Print ink reserve** · spheroid too big for the tip = **warn, never block** · presets = **free-form spins + a savable preset library**. Convention: total = barrel + tip, additive; `length_inches` keeps meaning the BARREL (a capillary stores mm/25.4) so `length_mm` and every barrel-length consumer are untouched; `total_length_mm = length_mm + tip_length_mm`. **THE CORE DECISION — `cross_section_area_mm2` is REDEFINED to the ORIFICE area** (+ a new explicit `barrel_area_mm2`): every external reader of it — the F-1 bead model in `FlowPhysics`/`GeometryEngine`/`SketchTrajectory`/Quick Print, the bore-column volumes in `PickAndPlaceManager`, the four workflow `_bore_area_mm2()` helpers — means "the bore the material passes through", which is the tip, so redefining is the FAIL-SAFE direction: a call site missed in the sweep gets the RIGHT number, whereas keeping barrel meaning would make any missed site silently wrong by ~1000× on a capillary. Barrel dims keep their legacy names (`id_um`/`od_um`/`length_mm` = what the operator types and every legacy readout prints); `orifice_*` aliases exist for readable new code; duck-typing-tolerant free functions (`needle_orifice_area_mm2`/`needle_flow_segments`/`needle_bore_profile`/`needle_orifice_od_mm`/`needle_particle_ratio`) are used at every consumer because several sites pass needle-LIKE stubs exposing only `gauge`/`id_m`/`length_mm`. **(1) MAX FLOW — the safety-critical one:** Hagen–Poiseuille on one d and one L reported ~49000 µL/s for a 580 µm blank whose 30 µm tip actually ceilings at **0.80 µL/s** (the ceiling hard-clamps every pump move, so an over-pressure shatters a glass tip). NEW `FlowSegment` (frozen; `volume_uL`, `equivalent_area_mm2`, `resistance_factor` K with R = 128µ/π·ΣK — cylinder `K=L/d⁴`, cone `K=(L/3)(d1²+d1d2+d2²)/(d1³d2³)`, the exact conical-Poiseuille result which **reduces to the cylinder form at d1==d2**, test-locked) + `NeedleSpec.flow_segments()` as **the ONLY place the taper model is expressed**; `needle_resistance_factor`/`needle_hydraulic_resistance`/`needle_pressure_drop_Pa`/`limiting_flow_segment`; `max_safe_flow_rate_uL_s` → `Q_max = ΔP/R_total` and `calculate_flow_safety` sums segments. Reynolds, **wall shear** (under-stated by (D1/D2)³ before → the cell-viability check silently passed) and the **clogging ratio** (a 1 mm barrel with a 30 µm tip was classified "free flow" for a 200 µm spheroid; now JAMMING) all switch to the ORIFICE. Cylinder is the default profile: it matches how a pull is specified, it is CONSERVATIVE on pressure (higher resistance for the same D2/L2 → ceiling errs low, the safe direction for glass), and it makes lift↔volume piecewise-LINEAR. **(2) BEAD:** deposited volume-per-mm + line spacing + closure overlap + the sketch bead band + the needle markers all follow the tip (a 1 mm barrel OD would give a 1 mm raster pitch for a 30 µm bead — fills as disconnected stripes). **(3) SPHEROID:** NEW frozen `BoreProfile(barrel_area, tip_area, tip_length)` with the **piecewise** `lift(V) = V/A2` for `V ≤ A2·L2` else `L2 + (V−A2·L2)/A1` (collapsing to the single-area form with no tip = byte-identical legacy); executor gains `bore_profile` + `_bore()`/`_lift_for_uL`/`_uL_for_lift` replacing two bare divisions in `_planned_aspirate_uL` and the disengage recompute — `_bore()` returns **None** (not a zero-area profile) when unusable, which is what preserves the "no bore ⇒ carrier volume, no wait" contract; a tapered tip uses its **equal-VOLUME cylinder area** (exact in volume, monotone, closed-form invertible — the exact frustum inverse is a cubic; the single documented approximation in the sink model). NEW `spheroid_pickup_detail` (too_large / tight / **past_tip**) — there was **NO fit check anywhere before**, and per the operator every severity is ADVISORY. The sink store's docstring premise ("the curve is a property of spheroid+fluid, a needle change only warns") is **FALSE for a capillary** — wall drag differs by orders of magnitude between a 250 µm tip and a 1 mm barrel and the curve becomes piecewise at L2 — corrected, with `tip_id_um`/`tip_length_mm`/`tip_area_mm2`/`tip_profile`/`needle_type` now stored beside the curve (written only when supplied → a straight-needle save is the identical JSON entry). **(4) VOLUME:** `internal_volume_uL` = barrel + tip (unchanged for a straight needle); NEW `ink_reserve_volume_uL`. ⚠ **The operator's tip-only reserve needed a guard**: a 30 µm × 5 mm tip holds ~3.5 nL, far below one pump step, so a literal tip-only reserve would put the buffer plug at the orifice the instant a print starts — the reserve is tip volume **when a tip exists, else the full bore volume** (so every straight needle keeps today's ~3.4 µL) and Quick Print warns via `_reserve_warning` below `_MIN_MEANINGFUL_RESERVE_UL` so the operator raises the ink padding instead. **UI:** Needle page gains a **needle-type combo** (Hypodermic (gauge) / Pulled glass capillary) switching between the untouched legacy gauge+length row and a new capillary card (barrel ID/OD/length, tip ID/OD/length, tip-profile combo; ranges deliberately WIDER than the common band because a clipping spin corrupts data silently while `validate()` can warn); channels clamp to 1 (single-bore); two-stage info label with per-stage µL; NEW **`SupportClasses/NeedleTypeStore.py`** (mirrors `WellTypeStore` — builtin/user dirs, user shadows builtin, atomic write, GUI-free, AST-locked) + 3 bundled pull recipes, with **STAMP-don't-reference** provenance (`needle_type_id` is never resolved at load, and the picker flips to "(custom)" the moment geometry diverges, so editing/deleting a preset can never mutate a saved setup or invalidate a calibration). **Persistence:** `to_dict` emits exactly the 7 legacy keys in their original order and appends a new key only when non-default; `from_dict` now FILTERS unknown keys instead of `cls(**data)` — the old form raised TypeError on any unrecognised key and, since `settings["hardware_config"]` is a full `to_dict()` mirror restored inside a broad `except` in `gui/app.py`, an older build reading a capillary settings.json would have silently lost the ENTIRE hardware config. `max_safe_flow_rate_uL_s` keeps a **single-cylinder fast path evaluating the ORIGINAL expression in the ORIGINAL operand order** — not an optimisation but the byte-identity guarantee (the two algebraic forms differ in the last ULP and the ceiling is stored/compared downstream); the test asserts `assertEqual`, not almost-equal. **Validation** branches (capillary checks tip ≤ barrel, positive lengths, OD > ID, single channel) while a gauge-less HYPODERMIC still reports the exact legacy `"No needle gauge selected"`; `__repr__` stops printing `NoneG`; `CalibrationSnapshotStore` gains `needle_type`/`needle_bore_um`/`needle_tip_length_mm` (gauge alone would fingerprint every capillary identically → a stale calibration silently trusted; and a pulled needle is barrel+tip LONG so a tip-length change invalidates the plate-bottom Z touch-off) **plus a required guard skipping dimensions absent from the saved fingerprint**, else every existing user gets a bogus "needle bore: None → 210" on first launch. **Deliberately NOT switched to the tip:** the optical µm/px calibration (`calibration.py:~14589`) MEASURES the needle silhouette — a 30 µm tip is ~20–40 px near the detector's floor and it will lock onto the in-focus barrel shoulder anyway, so it stays on the barrel OD (no behaviour change); a 30× error there would corrupt every spatial calibration invisibly. **Real numeric change by design:** the cell-removal/labeling bore columns now use the tip, so 0.1 mm through a 30 µm tip is ~0.07 nL (below one microstep). **THREE LATENT BUGS FIXED in the blast radius**, each reading `outer_diameter_um`/`inner_diameter_um` — names `NeedleSpec` has NEVER had: `jog_control.py:524` inside a swallowing `except`, so the jog workspace + XZ side view have **never** drawn a real needle diameter; `standard_jog_context.py` + `context_sections.py` silently dropped OD/ID so the needle readout on Jog, Calibration and all four pick&place workflows only ever showed the gauge (both now route through a shared `NeedleSpec.summary_line()`). Fixed here because they are 2-line changes on the identical lines this work touches, and shipping capillary support while that panel stays blind means jogging a 30 µm tip near glass with no tip geometry shown. **Also fixed:** `calibration.py` built text for `ctx_lbl_needle_info`, a widget created **nowhere in the repo** (its `hasattr` guard was always False) → dead block deleted; `wizard.py:386` assigned the catalog's OWN `NeedleSpec` instance into `config.needle` so a later edit mutated the shared catalog → now copies; `should_show_onboarding` gains a `hardware_config.needle` gate so a capillary-first operator (who never writes `workspace.needle_gauge`) isn't shown the wizard every launch; and `_apply_config_to_ui` no longer silently coerces a `length_inches` outside {1.0,1.5,2.0} to 1.0" on the next save. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (in order: 27G unchanged → capillary ceiling drops orders of magnitude + Quick Print speed auto-reduces → bead matches the tip → sink staircase linear through the L2 knee → tip-length edit fires the calibration-changed prompt). Tests: `tests/test_v76_pulled_capillary_needle.py` (**69, no skips** — byte-identity incl. every on-disk needle block, cone→cylinder reduction, bit-exact legacy ceiling, monotone-in-tip-Ø, cylinder≤cone conservatism, BoreProfile inverses/continuity/flattening, executor contracts, advisory-only feasibility, validation, fingerprint added-key guard, preset shadow/resurface/atomicity/GUI-free, GUI round-trips + provenance kept-vs-dropped, and all three readout bugfix regressions); existing bead/extrusion/max-flow/axis-speed/sink-timing/sketch/two-param/last-known-cal/context-panel/workflow-settings/cell/spheroid suites green. Two pre-existing failures on this branch confirmed NOT from this change (reproduced with it stashed): `test_v75x_pump_relief_and_bead_model` ×2 (suck-back, from uncommitted `PrintManager.py` WIP) and `test_v75x_print_setup_routine` ×2 (`_speed_pct_spin`, replaced by the v7.6 two-param rework). |
| `MEBP_v76_QUICKPRINT_TWO_PARAM_FEEDPLAN.md` | v7.6 **Quick Print driven by TWO parameters (resolution µm + top XY speed mm/s), the feed plan driving the REAL print, path overlays, never-stalling live position, and a hard abort** (operator: *"the user should set two parameters: the resolution they want, and the top speed of the XY stage … everything should calculate based on these parameters"*, + flow-limit/unattainable-resolution warnings, optional speed/flow/error/time overlays, real-time position that doesn't stall on axis motion or camera work, and *"abort print needs to knock out the motion of anything instantly and the needle should retract to a safe position"*). AskUserQuestion: the feed plan **drives the real velocity-mode print** (not just the preview) + the speed parameter is an **absolute mm/s** (old `speed_pct` profiles migrated). **(B) HARD ABORT — before v7.6 abort stopped NOTHING:** no XY stop, no pump stop, and a fire-and-forget `move_z_absolute` that was **not raise-only** (it DESCENDED when the needle was already above travel Z) issued **on the GUI thread**, able to freeze the UI 10–180 s behind an in-flight M400; `flush_moves`/`_wait_pump_move_complete`/`wait_for_z_arrival` were all abort-unaware. Now: NEW `ZPStage.quickstop()` (Marlin **M410**; lock acquired → `M410\n`, **contended → raw `\nM410\n` without the lock** — safe because the holder sits in its *read* loop, the leading newline terminates any partial line, and the OS queues each write whole; **self-healing**: with EMERGENCY_PARSER the M410 stops motion → the M400 completes → the blocked wait releases the lock, so the abort un-sticks the wait that was blocking it) + `resync_position()` (double M114 — M410 loses position accuracy and leaves a stray ok) + **EMERGENCY_PARSER parsed from M115 at connect** and logged by every abort (without it M410 queues → degrades to "no early stop", never worse than before); NEW `StageController.abort_all_motion(reason)` (Prior `I` + `VS 0,0` + quickstop + resync; any thread, never raises, ~2.5 s bounded); `abort_event=` (default None = byte-identical) threaded through `wait_for_z_arrival`/`_wait_pump_move_complete`/`_finish_pump_submove`/`move_pump_uL`/`_retract_z_slow_then_fast`/`ensure_retracted_to`/`safe_travel_to`, passed at the print's confirm/barrier/pump sites + all 29 PickPlace pump sites and its travel (fixing a 180 s preflight-abort latency) but **NEVER** by `_retract_to_safe_z` (the safety retract of record must always complete); `PrintManager.abort()` reworked → quick bookkeeping + a daemon `_abort_worker` (kill motion → join the print thread ≤20 s → backstop retract), GUI-thread Z move **deleted**; `_kick_abort_all_motion()` on all three Quick Print routes. **(A) FEED PLAN DRIVES THE REAL PRINT:** `PrintSettings.feed_plan_enabled/feed_plan_element_um/feed_plan_corner_split_deg` (defaults = legacy; top speed reuses `print_speed_mm_s` — a second speed field would desync `vol_per_mm=flow/print_speed`); `stamp_print_settings` also stamps `vel_tuning["tau_s"]` so the JOB carries a complete `StageCharacteristics` (plan reproducible from the job, no execute-time store read); `_feed_plan_char` gate → uncharacterised machine returns False **with no motion** and logs `feed_plan_fallback`; NEW `_execute_print_path_feed_plan` (build_plan at execute time, `path_start` gains feed_plan/n_sections/n_stops/plan_est_s/element_um/budget_um, per-section `plan_section` events, inter-section `VS 0,0` + 0.3 s dwell in 50 ms abort-checked chunks, tiny-section skip guard, and **GLOBAL pump bookkeeping** — `base_s` + monotone global `s` so deposited volume = `vol_per_mm × total` wherever the splits fall, nothing deposited during a stop, exactness top-up at the end) + `_run_plan_section` (legacy tick body parametrized per section with the **lag-aware end taper** and every legacy guard kept). `XYFeedPlan._tuning_for` → public **`tuning_for`** (+alias) and new **`section_end_taper`** so the print reuses the plan's own numbers. **(C) TWO PARAMETERS:** `_top_speed_spin` (mm/s) + `_resolution_spin` (µm) replace `_speed_pct_spin`; `_resolved_print_kinematics` = `min(top_speed, measured stage max, needle flow-limited max)` with the flow FOLLOWING the speed (volume-per-mm speed-invariant — changing speed changes TIME, never the bead); NEW dialog `migrate=` hook (applied in `apply()`, so last-used + profiles + imports all upgrade) → `_migrate_legacy_settings` carries `speed_pct` → mm/s (verified on the real profile: `speed_pct 2.0` → **0.12 mm/s**, faithful); NEW `XYFeedPlan.min_attainable_resolution_um` = arrive tol + post-arrival coast + encoder quantum = **23.0 µm** on ME3B V1 — ⚠ deliberately NOT scaled by `PREDICTION_HEADROOM` (that corrects *simulator* optimism; including it gave 36.8 µm, which would have warned that 30 µm is unattainable **on the machine that measured 1–27 µm at it**); warnings in `_refresh_setup_status`: flow ceiling / stage max (gated at ≥0.1 mm/s so an unmeasured stage can't print "max 0.00 mm/s") / resolution floor / uncharacterised machine / **time estimate** (`resolution 30 µm → est 42 s (12 corner stops)` — the accuracy-vs-time trade, priced). **(D) OVERLAYS:** `PrintTrajectoryMonitorView.set_overlay(mode, per_segment_values, unit, vmin, vmax)` colours the PREDICTED path by XY speed / flow / error / time (5-bucket ramp, legend in the readout band, planned-blue + executed-green untouched, mismatched counts fall back to dashed, a new prediction clears stale values) + pure `_overlay_channels` (speed=Δs/Δt, flow=speed×vol_per_mm, error=|cross| via bisect into `cross_profile`, time=global print clock) + 4-arg `predicted` bridge signal + exclusive pills; `_kick_prediction` now runs `build_plan`+`simulate_plan` (the same sections the print executes). **(E) LIVE POSITION NO LONGER FREEZES:** root cause was PRINT_PATH suspending the poller while the loop's own 25–31 Hz `cached=False` reads went only to the JSONL and never back-filled the cache → the monitor froze for the whole print; NEW `PositionPoller.note_xy`/`note_zp` + back-fill from `get_xy_position(cached=False)` unfreezes **every** cached reader with no new wiring (odometer + ZP liveness stay poll-thread-only so travel can't double-count), plus `_set_print_live(on)` with a **10 Hz** `_live_pos_timer` during prints and `set_throttled` halving the GUI-thread camera grab rate. Deferred: camera reader-thread offload (needs ToupCam/Andor affinity verification) and open-loop mode's marker (that mode does no position reads at all — a ~2 Hz read would fix it via the back-fill). **Bugs caught during implementation (all fixed):** a silently-swallowed `NameError` (`get_store` not imported at module scope) that disabled EVERY new warning while a test merely *skipped*; chunked abort sleeps bounded by wall clock spun ~37 M times under a patched `time.sleep` (pump suite 0.16 s → 301 s) — now bounded by chunk count with the legacy single-sleep path preserved exactly. **⭐ HARDWARE-VERIFIED 2026-07-29 — full instrumented star print, pumps excluded (operator: *"do not use the pumps, instead of the pump just control Z 5mm up and down … make a print object, and a print trajectory like a star. track the x,y, and z locations over time"*).** Machine-specific safety facts established BEFORE motion: `axis_map P1→X, P2→Y, P3→E` means **Marlin X/Y/E ARE THE PUMPS**, so the test commanded **Marlin Z only**; and because the calibrated max-retraction reference (45.0) sits only ~1 mm above the start (safe Z 44.0, envelope 0–60, plate bottom 21.13), the ±5 mm excursion was run **DOWN-then-back-up** (44→39→44, ~17.9 mm plate clearance) instead of up-first, which would have pushed toward a possible hard stop. The board did NOT lose position on connect (`_board_reset_detected: False`, raw −59.14 = zero-ref 44.0 = the saved value); re-declared via G92 anyway. **✅ HW-1 ANSWERED: `EMERGENCY_PARSER: True`** — this SKR Mini E3 V3 reports `Cap:EMERGENCY_PARSER:1`, so M410 executes on arrival and the abort's bounded-latency + self-healing-raw-write argument is real, not conditional. **Z (the pump substitute), staged 0.5→2→5 mm: every stage exact to 0.000 mm both directions**, returning to 44.000. **Star:** a real print object created through the normal path (**`HW_Test_Star_8mm`**, csv_import + CSV, in the Print Library), loaded back and driven at 3 mm/s / 30 µm element → plan **10 sections, 9 corner stops**; result **p95 25 µm · rms 11 µm · max 37 µm · completion 1.000 · PASS** vs the element; **Z range 0.0 µm over 206 samples across the 21 s path** (dead flat); both pump substitutes exactly **5.000 mm**; XY span **0.001 mm** during the Z moves; travel phase held Z 44.000 while XY moved 4.000 mm (retract-first, no descent); ended at safe Z **44.000** — i.e. **the axes are cleanly decoupled in both directions**. **✅ The live-position back-fill confirmed on hardware**: the sampler read X/Y from the poller CACHE while the follower did the fresh reads and the cache swept the full 7.61 mm of star extent during the path (before v7.6 it was frozen for the whole PRINT_PATH). **🔧 Defect found + fixed BY the run:** measured 1.31 s per corner stop (28.64 mm at 3 mm/s = 9.5 s motion, actual 21.4 s) against the 0.35 s `build_plan` assumed → the time estimate was **1.7× optimistic**, which matters because that estimate is how the operator prices "finer resolution costs time"; extracted as **`XYFeedPlan.STOP_COST_S = 1.3`** with the measurement recorded → the same star now estimates **21.2 s vs 21.4 s measured (−1 %)**, 42 feed-plan tests still green. Artefacts: `logs/hwtest/star_run_*.json`, `z_probe_*.json`, `v76_star_hw_run.png`. **⚠ MY ERROR IN THE FIRST TWO RUNS, disclosed:** the harness applied safety limits + the Z convention but **never called `apply_device_settings()`**, so the live ZPStage kept its DEFAULT axis map (`Z→X, P1→Y, …`) instead of this machine's (`Z→Z, P1→X, …`) — every "Z" command went to Marlin **X = pump P1**. The needle never moved (Marlin Z stayed at zero-ref 44.0) and **a pump did**, which the test was told not to touch; the moves were relative and net-zero (−5/+5, twice) so the plunger returned to its start inside P1's −30…0 envelope with no fluid net-displaced, and P1's corrupted counter was **restored to its pre-session −19.7 by a software G92 (no motion)** — all four axes then matched the saved `zp_last_position`. It looked perfect (0.000 mm errors) because write and read shared the same wrong map: **self-consistent and wrong**. Now enforced by four pre-motion guards (push `apply_device_settings`, assert live map == profile map, assert logical Z's Marlin letter is not a pump's, snapshot pump counters and assert zero drift at the end). **✅ ALL-AXES-SIMULTANEOUS re-run** (operator: *"during a print all axis must move simultaneously"* — the first runs bracketed the Z excursion before/after the path, which is sequential; a print advances the pump *while* XY moves, so Z is now driven from the follower's **per-Δs deposit hook**, a triangle 44.0→39.0→44.0 across the 28.6 mm star, 293 non-blocking increments): **of 237 XY-moving samples, 192 (81 %) had Z moving too**, Z 0.79 mm/s while XY ran 2.27 mm/s, Z tracked its commanded profile to **p50 5 µm / p95 22 µm**, travelled 4.980 of 5.000 mm, **and XY was NOT degraded — p95 26 µm · rms 11 · completion 1.000 · PASS** (vs 27 µm sequential, inside noise) with pump drift 0.000. The residual 19 % is Z's 0.02 mm increment quantisation + the apex reversal, not sequencing. Figures `logs/hwtest/v76_simultaneous_axes.png` + `v76_star_hw_run.png`. ⚠ **Honest caveat:** the feed plan deliberately STOPS XY at each sharp corner (9 here) and, since Z/pump advance ∝ XY progress, all axes pause together there — that is the mechanism buying ≤30 µm. A truly never-stopping print is the opposite end of the same trade (set `corner_split_deg` > 180 to disable splitting) and restores the ~220 µm corner cut; flagged for an operator decision, not silently chosen. **⭐ END-TO-END QUICK PRINT THROUGH THE PRODUCTION EXECUTOR — 2026-07-29** (operator: *"this should be testing the print with the simulated trajectory that we are making via quick print … make a quick print and oversee the process so that we can test the code on real hardware and compare what we think is going to happen with actual performance … instead of using the pump axis we should use the z axis … at the end, i want a pannel of real vs simulated vs ideal"*) — closes the "drove `run_plan` directly" gap: the whole production chain ran, nothing re-implemented — `QuickPrintWorkflowPage._build_settings` (real stamping; `__new__` partial page + stub spins so the GUI isn't booted against live HW) → `build_well_plate_job` (7 cmds, `return_home=False`) → `PrintManager.start` → confirmed descent → prime → `_execute_print_path_feed_plan` → `_run_plan_section` ×10 → suckback → retract, with `build_plan`+`simulate_plan` captured **before** the run so the comparison is a prediction not a postdiction. **PUMPS NOT USED via ONE choke point** (not per-call-site patches): `ctrl.move_pump_uL` → accumulate signed volume + NON-BLOCKING `move_z_absolute` hard-clamped to [39,44] (so Z advances *while* XY moves, the pump's contract) and `ctrl.move_pump_relative` → refused+logged (never reached). A plunger advances monotonically, so Z descends 44.0→39.0 across the path and the job's own `TRAVEL_UP` restores 44.0; **print Z set EQUAL to travel/safe Z (44.0)** so the needle never descends toward the plate. Two NEW pre-motion guards beyond the four above: a `_feed_plan_char` pre-check (**an incomplete stamp silently falls back to the legacy follower — that would not be a feed-plan test at all**) and an XY-envelope fit check that refuses rather than trace a clamped edge. A **dry run on the simulators first** caught two harness faults free (sim's persisted Z can't reach print height → G92 re-datum; my JSONL parser keyed `event` when the field is `ev`, which made the run look like it logged nothing). **Result:** COMPLETED; `path_start feed_plan=true, n_sections=10, n_stops=9`; **10 `plan_section` events** with global `base_s_mm` 0→25.78; `path_end status=arrived, s_mm=28.644 == tot_mm`; **0 fallback**; wall **21.7 s vs 21.2 s estimated (+2 %)** — the corrected `STOP_COST_S` holds on a second independent path; 156 Z increments = 3.9377 µL ≙ 5 mm, 0 clamps; **pump drift 0.000**; ended at 44.000. **simulated p95 7 / rms 2 / max 9 µm vs REAL p95 27 / rms 12 / max 36 µm, both completion 1.000, both PASS vs 30 µm**; an independent 20 Hz **cached**-position sampler agreed at p95 26 µm (back-fill again keeping the GUI live through a real PRINT_PATH). Panel `logs/hwtest/v76_quickprint_panel.png`, data `quickprint_hw_20260729_121442.json`. **🔎 The comparison's real lesson: the error is section RESTARTS, not corner cutting** — decomposed ±0.5 mm about the splits, real p95 **31.7 µm near a section end vs 17.8 µm mid-section** (sim 7.7 / 2.4); the vertex itself is *hit* (what the stop buys), each peak is the next section spinning up and it decays before the following corner, and the real stage carries a ~6 µm mid-section floor the FOPDT model has no term for. **⚠ FINDING NEEDING A DECISION — `PREDICTION_HEADROOM = 2.0` is not an upper bound:** real/sim p95 = **3.75×** here, and re-deriving from both stored HW panels (rather than trusting the earlier summary) the tuning-only panel medians **1.55×** (4/17 over 2.0) while on the planned panel the **curved** shapes are consistently optimistic — Circle 10 mm **4.28×**, Star 10 mm 3.26×, Star 5 mm 3.21×, Circle 5 mm 3.18×, Circle 2 mm 3.16× (5/18 over 2.0) — and the polygons pessimistic (Square/Zigzag/Comb 0.13–0.25×). So 3.75× is **consistent with prior HW data, not an anomaly**, and 2.0 is a middling figure used as a safety factor: a shape predicted at 16 µm is called PASS for a 30 µm element yet could measure ~60 µm. Raising it (≈4.0 for curved geometry) would demand predicted ≤ 7.5 µm — safer but it will flag designs that would print. **Deliberately NOT changed unilaterally** (trades false-pass against false-fail). **Still outstanding:** Prior `I` mid-VS + a deliberate abort drill (M410 stop latency, raw-write-during-M400) — not triggered during clean runs; corner blobs with real ink; the `PREDICTION_HEADROOM` decision; and a run with the **real pumps** (the executor + bookkeeping are now proven; the plunger, its compliance and corner ooze are not). Tests: `test_v76_hard_abort.py` (28, incl. the descend-hazard regression + contended-quickstop no-deadlock) + `test_v76_feed_plan_print.py` (25, incl. flag-off byte-identity + **volume conservation** + tiny-section skip) + `test_v76_two_param_and_overlays.py` (44); regression 245 (challenge/velocity/open-loop/confirmed/auto-cal/pick-place/cell/logging) + 129 (ZP/jog/safe-Z/seam/stress/jog-nav) + 163 (v7.6 + simulator) green. One pre-existing failure confirmed NOT from this change: `test_v75x_zp_auto_reconnect_and_fast_z::test_no_z_max_keeps_defaults` (200 vs 500 from `get_max_z_feedrate_mm_min`; present in HEAD, zero hits in this diff, already documented below). |
| `MEBP_v75x_XY_CHALLENGE_UPGRADE.md` | v7.5.x **XY Printing Challenge upgrade — the bench that tunes the closed-loop velocity follower was producing wrong answers.** Operator: *"the tuning sweep is just bringing the velocity down … we want to keep the velocity of the stage and only do the corner slowdowns"* + *"every time I autotune I get different answers"*. **Root causes, all verified numerically against this machine's stored calibration + the operator's own sweep:** (1) **`lookahead` IS the speed knob** — `speed_cap = lookahead/((loop+phase)·safety)`, so the tuner's lookahead grid sweeps speed 0.314 → 2.038 mm/s and minimising path error minimises *speed*; the sweep's RMS 8 → 104 µm tracks that exactly, and 3 of 4 descent-tuned params sit on a grid endpoint. (2) **Corner slowdown is provably INERT** — at `print_speed=5` the corner limits span 1.00–4.25 mm/s, all above the 0.314 cap, so `min(cap, corner_limit)` is always the cap (hence the operator's RMS 8 µm flat across the whole `corner_speed_factor` grid); "slow only at corners" was unreachable until the cap was fixed. (3) The cap is driven by **one bad sample**: `get_phase_lag_s()` averages `by_phase.intercept_s` = 0.2872 s, 90 % of the dead-time budget, whose dominant 0.5766 s term is a **single-point fit of one run** and whose set includes two physically impossible negatives — capping prints at **0.31 mm/s on a stage measured at 5.9 mm/s** (`slope_s_per_seg` is NOT the fix: seconds *per segment*, a unit error). (4) **Runs that never finished won** — every best-scoring run logged `wall-time cap hit`, was truncated, and was scored anyway. (5) **The metric is blind**: one-sided/unsigned/untimed over visited samples only, so the logged deadlock (12.4 mm travelled for 1.0 mm net over 37 s) and an exact retrace both score **~0 µm**. **Landed (Stages 0–2):** `tests/support/store_fixture.py` fixing two singleton-store leaks (one restored `TCS._STORE`, a name that does not exist); **golden legacy-identity tests** pinning `pursuit_step`/`resolve_control`/`plan_speed_limits` to 1e-12 before any edit, plus this machine's 0.3136 mm/s cap and the inert-corner proof; the dialog's **seven parallel param structures collapsed into one declarative `PARAM_SPEC`** (needed a `grid_modes` field or the shared corner-angle spec leaked its grid into the velocity sweep). `resolve_control` gains `dead_time_s`/`lead_time_frac`/`min_lookahead_frac`/`max_speed_frac`/`hold_speed` (all 0 = legacy) and now returns the **resolved** lookahead + `cap_reason` naming the binding term; new **`SupportClasses/XYDeadTime.py`** measures the real command→motion dead time by step response (±X/±Y/±diag, medians+MAD, FOPDT cruise fit giving an independent top-speed cross-check; always stops the stage, returns to start, never touches Z, poller suspended, envelope-clamped); store gains `get/set_velocity_dead_time_s` + `effective_dead_time_s()` and ~25 velocity keys all defaulting to 0; `PrintSettings.vel_tuning` (**one dict** instead of ~25 scalars) + `xy_jerk_pct` + `velocity_tuning()`, consumed by `_execute_print_path_velocity` (resolved lookahead, jerk, `cap_reason`/`dead_time_s`/`tuning` logged in `path_start`) and stamped by Quick Print; the bench's `_resolve` makes the **same call with the same arguments** so tuning still transfers. New **speed-budget readout** + **"Measure dead time"** button: on the operator's numbers it reads *"commanded 0.31 mm/s — limited by dead_time … ⚠ Corner tuning is INERT here"*, and after storing a measured 45 ms it reads 1.30 mm/s (4.1×), or 5.00 mm/s with `hold_speed`. **Honest scoring** in `XYChallenge.py`: `Sample` (tuple subclass → zero consumer churn), an **exact** `_SegGrid` spatial index (proved equal to the retained brute force), `signed_cross_track`/`two_sided_deviation`/`coverage`/`completion_fraction`/`path_length_ratio` (the dither detector)/`reversal_count`/`corner_overshoot`/`along_track_lag`/`throughput`/`dwell_events` → `path_report` (keeping `legacy` for comparability) → `composite_score` with **graded** hard failures (`1000 + penalty`, not `inf`, so descent keeps a gradient out of the failing region it currently ships in) + `verdict_for` requiring both RMS and max; new shapes **Line-Reversal** / **Comb** / **Dwell-Stitch** + `SHAPE_PARAMS`/`shape_meta`. Caught a real bug while validating: `completion_fraction` used a global projection, so on a **closed** shape the closing point projected to `s=0` and a *perfect* run read 98.8 % complete → every perfect run would have hard-failed; fixed with a forward-windowed monotone cursor. **Verified:** deadlock/retrace/half-path all FAIL with the right reasons while `path_error` reports ~0; 25 µm passes, 150 µm fails, a 10× slower run scores worse. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (verify `VS` direction on a low safe path FIRST → measure dead time → confirm corner tuning finally *does* something with `hold_speed` → bench-vs-print `cross_um` overlay). **PID tuner fix** (operator: *"the PID tuner is not doing a good job"* — `pid_kp` walked 4.938 → 6.216 → 6.763 → 7.052 across consecutive presses): the cross-track plant is a **pure integrator with transport delay** (a perpendicular velocity command integrates directly into cross-track position), so `Ku = π/(2L)` and `Tu = 4L` are **analytic** — the relay was measuring something computable. NEW `VelocityControl.plant_ultimate_gain` / `pid_gains_from_dead_time` (exactly repeatable, no stage motion) / `relay_sanity` (a measured `Ku` above `π/(2L)` is physically impossible → gate + fallback), a new **"PID from dead time"** button, and three relay defects fixed in `_worker_zn`: **no hysteresis** on the sign switch (so it chattered on µm quantisation near the line, manufacturing spurious tiny half-cycles), the amplitude was the **mean** over half-cycles (which those cycles dragged down — and `Ku ∝ 1/a`, so an under-measured amplitude *inflates* the gain), and no repeat/sanity check → now a hysteresis band scaled off the resolution element, the **median of per-cycle peaks** with transients dropped, ≥6 flips, and the physical gate. `kd`/`ki` now deliberately 0 with the reasoning recorded (unfiltered D on a µm-quantised 25 Hz encoder adds noise; the plant is already an integrator so P alone has zero steady-state error and a second integrator is a classic limit-cycle source — `ki` was previously discarded by accident). **`pid_kp` descent grid widened `[0,0.5,1,2,4]` → `[0,1,2,3.5,5,7,9]`: the correct gain for this machine is ≈5.24, ABOVE the old grid's maximum, so the descent was structurally unable to find it.** Model validated by the operator's own relay run — it measured `Tu = 0.391 s` vs the predicted 0.396 s (~1 %) while its `Ku` was 1.19× the physical maximum, which is exactly the signature of the amplitude bug. **Also confirmed on real HW: "Measure dead time" returned 67 ms** against the 287 ms `by_phase` was claiming — a 4.3× correction that raises the speed cap AND moves the correct `kp` from 1.8 to 5.24. **⭐ ROOT CAUSE OF THE DEADLOCK FOUND + FIXED — the projection window.** Surfaced while bench-simulating the one-click calibrator: derived settings failed verification at 2 mm/s but PASSED at 3 mm/s, and slower-should-be-easier means bug, not tuning. `pursuit_step` bounds the projection search to `max_ds = max(0.15, speed·dt·6)` and `project_on_polyline` broke its scan as soon as the next segment's far end exceeded that window — so **when the window is smaller than the local segment only the CURRENT segment is ever examined**, `s` pins at that segment's end, `seg_i` never advances, the carrot stops, and the stage sits there while the cross-track term thrashes it. The operator's numbers fit exactly: 0.996 mm/s @ 25 Hz ⇒ window **0.239 mm** against a `seg_mm_mean` of **0.4859 mm**; the log froze at `s = 0.509 mm` ≈ one segment, with 12.4 mm travelled for 1.0 mm net. Fix = `MIN_SEGMENTS_EXAMINED = 2` (always examine the segment AFTER the cursor); two segments is nothing like a spiral revolution so loop-snap protection is untouched, and the per-tick COMMITTED advance is still bounded by `max_ds` so pump deposition + the snap guard are unchanged (both asserted; the existing spiral no-snap tests still pass). **⚠ a deliberate default-path behaviour change** — the old behaviour is a deadlock. In simulation every shape × speed now completes (`completion 1.000`, dither 0.9–1.0) where it previously froze at ~0.1 completion with dither 40–75; residual error is now pure-pursuit **corner cutting** scaling with the lookahead (p95 117 µm @ 0.34 mm → 587 µm @ 1.68 mm), which is exactly what the withheld cross-track gain removes. **ALL-IN-ONE ONE-CLICK CALIBRATION** (operator: *"does everything in the XY timing calibration in one shot … applied for all prints … a systematic one-click process"*): the insight is that almost nothing needs SEARCHING — three measurements (loop period, dead time, top speed) plus closed-form physics fix everything else, so the same machine measured twice yields the same settings. NEW pure **`SupportClasses/XYAutoCalibration.py`** (`MeasuredMachine`/`CalibrationPolicy`/`derive_settings` where every value carries a `Note` with its reasoning/`validate`/dependency-ordered `CALIBRATION_STEPS`), deriving `lookahead = target·L·safety·headroom` (**inverted**, so the lookahead is sized FROM the desired speed instead of dictating it), `pid_kp` from `Ku = π/(2L)`, `decel ≥ coast + ramp`, `d_filter_hz`, `settle_tol` and the guard values; `lookahead_margin`=1.5 because solving for the target puts the operating point exactly ON the stability boundary (a bench sweep showed that dithers). Wired to a **⚙ Calibrate XY (one click)** button → centre → comms → dead time → derive → apply → verify on Square+Star with the completion/dither-aware score. **Operator requirement — never reach the travel extents:** every probe moves RELATIVE to its start and a clamped probe fails silently (it measures the clamp), so `envelope_center_um`/`usable_test_radius_um`/`check_fits`/`fit_shape_size_mm`/`center_stage` centre the stage in the reachable envelope first, shrink any excursion that will not fit with a 2 mm margin, and refuse when the envelope is unknown; `XYDeadTime` centres by default and shape sizes auto-shrink; centring goes through `safe_travel_to(..., target_z_mm=None)` so Z retracts first and never descends. **"Applied to ALL prints" was FALSE — gap closed:** only Quick Print stamped the tuning, **Full Print stamped none of it**, so a bench session had no effect there; one shared `stamp_print_settings` is now called by both `quick_print_workflow._build_settings` and `print_setup_legacy._get_settings`. **Gain safety gate:** `VC.SUPPORTS_BOUNDED_CROSS_TRACK=False` makes `derive_settings` emit `pid_kp = 0` (pure pursuit) while the law still adds the PD to a full-magnitude vector, reporting the analytic gain it *would* use and why — an automated calibration must not hand the machine a gain the law mishandles; flip with Stage 3.1. Also corrected a derivation error caught in simulation: `pace_correction` was top-speed ÷ the dead-time probe's cruise, but that probe raises SMS to ~1.5× its own step so its cruise reads back the PROBE speed (it read 1.98 purely because the probe ran at 3 mm/s against a 5.9 mm/s stage) — now left to the dedicated sweep. Derived for ME3B V1 @ 5 mm/s (L = 99 ms): lookahead 0.2→**1.49**, control_hz 25→**31.5**, decel 0.3→**0.565** (0.3 was SHORTER than the 495 µm coast ⇒ endpoint overshoot), hold_speed off→**on**, max_speed_frac→**0.9**. **Remaining (Stages 3–7):** bounded cross-track authority + re-acquire + stall/dither guards (**the accuracy step — corner cutting is now the dominant error**), the derivative filter (`d_filter_hz` stored but unconsumed; required before `kd` can be non-zero), lead predictor, curvature-aware speed profile, shared `build_follower`/`follower_tick`, bench JSONL + run history, the repeatable tuner, and the robustness panel (**it still persists nothing — its results exist only in the in-memory log pane**). Tests: `test_v75x_xy_challenge_metrics.py` (42) + `test_v75x_xy_dead_time.py` (22) + `test_v75x_pid_from_dead_time.py` (20) + `test_v75x_xy_auto_calibration.py` (45) + challenge suites **192**; regression 125 + 180 → **497 green**. **REAL-HW SESSION (2026-07-28, ME3B V1, XY-only on COM6 — COM4/Marlin never opened):** `VS` direction VERIFIED correct on all four axes (the long-standing caveat, settled); measured loop 32.0 ms / dead time 67.1 ms ± 0.34 (apparent, through the read path; transport ~40 ms) / τ 27.1 ms / cruise 2996 vs 3000 commanded — all persisted to the store; verification runs Circle p95 84 µm & Star p95 364 µm both completion 1.000 at 3 mm/s with the derived lookahead 0.892. Hazard caught: a fresh `StageController`'s DEFAULT ±130000 limits put the "centre" at the real envelope's CORNER — load real `safety_limits` before any envelope-relative probe; `center_stage` now VERIFIES arrival. **🐞 NEW BUG diagnosed (NOT fixed): Prior ack corruption** — `send_command` never drains the Prior's `R` ack (it answers `R` to everything incl. `SMS`/`SAS`), the stale `R` is consumed by the next `P` read (`Expected 3 values, got 1: R`, present in the operator's own app.log) → `wait_for_xy_arrival` polls garbage and times out though the stage arrived, undermining confirm-mode + point-to-point; a naive write+drain fix made reads come back EMPTY on real HW and was REVERTED (investigation note in `XYStage._send_protocol_command`; `send_command(drain_ack=)` left in, default off) — needs a raw-serial bench session; the comms-rate checker is suspect for the same reason. **STAGE MODEL + SIMULATOR + GEOMETRY PANEL + PRINTABILITY (2026-07-28 second HW session):** the measured dynamics are now a SAVED artefact — new pure `SupportClasses/XYStageModel.py` (`StageCharacteristics.from_store/to_dict` + a FOPDT `XYStageModel`: dead-time command PIPELINE (not a resettable timer — an earlier ad-hoc sim got that wrong and froze), first-order τ lag, top-speed clamp, quantised readout) + `SupportClasses/XYPathSimulator.py` with **ONE `follow_path` loop over injected `FollowIO` bindings** — an exact transcription of the bench `_drive_velocity` tick — bound to the model with a virtual clock (`model_io`, deterministic) or to the real controller (`controller_io`), so sim ≡ hw in loop logic by construction; `simulate_follow`→`SimResult` (report/score/verdict/signed cross profile/`fail_regions` in world coords), `tuning_from_store`, `simulate_panel`, `printing_subpaths_from_trajectory` (Nx7 → printing sub-paths, `_travel_mask` rule, weld/Z-lift safe) + `check_toolpath/check_trajectory`. GUI: `gui/dialogs/xy_geometry_panel_dialog.py` (shape×size matrix, ideal grey vs simulated dashed vs hardware solid coloured by verdict; Simulate = instant/no motion, Run-on-stage = centred + poller/watchdog suspended + poll-verified gotos + VS 0,0 finally; persists/reloads `logs/challenge/geometry_panel_*.json`; launched from the challenge dialog's **📐 Geometry panel…**) and `gui/dialogs/sketch_printability_dialog.py` + **🎯 Check printability** on Print Builder → Sketch (splits the compiled trajectory, simulates each printing sub-path with saved characteristics + current tuning, rings the predicted fail/warn regions, per-path "stalls at 38%" detail, speed spin re-simulates instantly; verdicts use `PREDICTION_HEADROOM`=2.0). **VALIDATED on an 18-cell real panel (6 shapes × 2/5/10 mm) driven through the SAME shipped `follow_path`: 16/18 verdict agreement, ALL 4 real stalls predicted (wall-times to ~0.1 s), median actual/predicted p95 = 1.56 → the 2.0 headroom; headline: Star 2 mm predicted deadlock (p95 1111, wall-cap) → actual deadlock (p95 1154, dither 22.8)** — a hardware failure called before the stage moved. Two operational findings (sim AND hw): **180° reversals stall the velocity follower** (all Line-Reversal cells; the sketch back-trace/retrace features produce exactly this geometry → velocity-mode retraced prints will stall until Stage 3.3 lands; the checker flags it at design time), and **feature size ≲ 2× lookahead risks a pursuit orbit at low speed** (Star 2 mm @ 0.892 lookahead deadlocked). Tests: `test_v75x_xy_stage_model.py` (14) + `test_v75x_xy_path_simulator.py` (24, incl. the hardware-verified operating point pinned in a loose band) + `test_v75x_geometry_panel_and_printability.py` (11); challenge/calibration regression 195 + sketch-page 21 green. **⭐ FEED PLANNER — 18/18 SHAPES ≤ 30 µm ON REAL HARDWARE (2026-07-28 second panel; operator: "we know the print path a priori … get all 18 shapes under 30 microns"):** derived-optimal TUNING alone still failed 16/18 in sim (corner cut ≈ 0.4·lookahead and reversal stalls are structural), so NEW pure **`SupportClasses/XYFeedPlan.py`** builds a deterministic, feature-aware plan FROM the path: sharp corners (>55°) + 180° reversals become section SPLITS (decelerate, stop ON the vertex at 10 µm arrive + settle dwell, continue as a fresh section → zero corner cut / zero reversal stall by construction); curved sections get a curvature-sized lookahead `la=0.8·√(2·R·δ)` (pursuit standoff la²/2R inverted for budget δ=element/1.6) whose dead-time stability rule sets the section speed `v=la/(L·safety)` (2 mm circle → 0.79 mm/s; straights → full speed); `build_plan` (per-section reasoning strings) / `run_plan` (sections = consecutive `follow_path` runs over the same injected I/O — sim ≡ hw) / `simulate_plan` (scored vs the FULL path) / `plan_rows` (time-matched X/Y/Z/pump rows, volume ∝ arc length, stop dwells — the deterministic all-axis trajectory). Enabling fix: **lag-aware end taper** in `follow_path` (opt-in `arrive_mm`/`end_lag_s`/`end_floor_frac`, defaults = legacy) — the naive `remaining/decel` taper commanded speeds for where the stage WAS a dead-time ago → crossed section ends at ~1.5 mm/s and overshot a measured **153 µm**; the taper now subtracts MEASURED velocity (successive reads; commanded under-predicts during braking) × lag, floor ~80 µm/s → coast ≈ 8 µm. **Planned HW panel (`geometry_panel_planned_20260728_172210.json`): 18/18 ≤ 30 µm, actual p95 1–27 µm — Star 2 mm 1154 µm DEADLOCK → 7 µm, Square 2 mm 348 → 1 µm, Line-Reversal STALL → 1 µm; sim predicted 18/18 first (p95 0–16); stops cost ~10–20 % wall time.** Figures: `logs/challenge/geometry_panel_planned_*.png` + `geometry_panel_before_after.png`; renderer `tools_render_geometry_panel.py` (repo root; newest panel default; `MEBP_CHALLENGE_LOG_DIR` redirects panel records in tests). **Quick Print:** `PrintTrajectoryMonitorView.set_predicted_path(segments, note)` (dashed peach simulated stage path, in the auto-fit bbox) + live **progress readout** (`print 42% · 12.3/29.0 mm · dev 38 µm` — forward-only `project_on_polyline` cursor over the plan while recording); `QuickPrintWorkflowPage._kick_prediction` simulates on a background thread after every plan refresh (velocity mode + measured machine only; generation-tagged) via new `_PrintBridge.predicted`; motion-mode combo refreshes it. Wiring the FeedPlan into the print EXECUTOR is the tracked follow-up (pump semantics need their own pass). Tests: `test_v75x_xy_feed_plan.py` (17) + `test_v75x_quick_print_predicted_overlay.py` (8). |
| `MEBP_v75x_STAGE_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD.md` | v7.5.x **fix — "the camera freezes whenever I use any other stages in jog mode."** Follow-on to `MEBP_v75x_PUMP_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD.md` (pump was already off-thread): the **XY/Z** incremental-jog handlers `HardwareControlPanel._on_jog_xy`/`_on_jog_z` (the jog-button-array, used on Jog / Calibration / jog workflows) still ran on the GUI thread — the move (`move_xy_relative_um`/`move_z_user_relative`, a serial round-trip; Z is the Marlin `ok`-handshaked write) **plus** `_force_refresh_positions()` doing **two** blocking `cached=False` serial reads (Prior `P` + Marlin `M114`, contended with the poller) — freezing the Qt event loop so no `QTimer` camera feed could tick. The button jog was the worst case (move + two fresh reads). **Fix:** new `_run_stage_jog(busy_attr, move_fn, label)` runs the speed-set + move + the fresh `get_xy_position`/`get_zp_position(cached=False)` reads on a daemon thread (serial access is `_serial_lock`-protected), then hands the results to the GUI thread via a new queued `_stage_jog_done(object, object)` signal → `_on_stage_jog_done` paints the readout with **no serial I/O on the GUI thread**; per-axis busy guards (`_xy_jog_busy`/`_z_jog_busy`) drop overlapping clicks (independent, so XY isn't blocked by an in-flight Z). Jog behaviour + signal contract unchanged. Keyboard jog (`jog_control._key_jog_*`, move-only, no fresh reads) left on the GUI thread (much cheaper; key auto-repeat wants tight coupling) — documented follow-up. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1/V3** (jog XY/Z while watching a live camera feed — no freeze; readout still updates). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_stage_jog_off_gui_thread.py` (3: XY off-thread + overlapping-click dropped + fresh reads off-thread; Z off-thread; independent guards) + updated `test_v75x_axis_max_speed_inputs.py` (wait for the backgrounded `set_velocity`); jog/speed/context regression 136 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_QUICK_PRINT_CONFIRMED_SEGMENTS.md` | v7.5.x **fix — Quick Print ink laydown XY trajectory "very wrong" (smeared) while pickup/wash were fine → CLOSED-LOOP velocity following** (operator, 2026-07-27). Root-caused from the operator's own run `logs/prints/print_20260727_171512_..._B6.jsonl`: PRINT_PATH streams each XY segment OPEN-LOOP (`move_xy_absolute` fire-and-forget, paced only by `time.sleep(seg_len/print_speed)`, nothing waits for arrival), but the Prior stage runs **~2.5× slower** than commanded (`SMS 11%`, measured `max=22282 µm/s`; optimistic SMS%↔speed cal + short ~0.48 mm accel-dominated segments), so the physical stage falls PROGRESSIVELY behind the stream — sampled `lag_um` grew **0 → 6144 (median) → 10736 (max) µm** across a ~10 mm print, end-of-path drain **timed out 30 s still 2.09 mm short** — while the pump extrudes on the fast software schedule → smeared pattern. Pickup/wash are point-to-point `safe_travel_to` (WAIT for arrival → slow≠wrong); PRINT_PATH is the only open-loop path (slow→wrong). **Two executor modes added to `_execute_print_path`, both gated by new `PrintSettings` flags (default False = legacy open-loop, so Full Print / Print Setup unchanged):** **(1) `velocity_follow` — CLOSED-LOOP velocity following, the Quick Print default (operator asked for continuous velocity correction, NOT stop-and-go):** NEW `_execute_print_path_velocity` runs a ~25 Hz (`_VEL_CONTROL_HZ`) loop that each tick polls the ACTUAL position (`get_xy_position(cached=False)`; poller already suspended for PRINT_PATH), projects it onto the toolpath → real **arc-length** progress `s` (`project_on_polyline`), deposits pump volume ∝ REAL `Δs` (`vol_per_mm=flow/print_speed`; non-blocking, no M400 in-loop), and commands a **velocity vector** (`send_velocity_xy`→Prior `VS,vx,vy` µm/s) toward a **carrot** placed `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM` ahead of `s` (pure pursuit), ramping down over the last `_VEL_DECEL_MM`. Carrot tied to REAL progress ⇒ it can never run ahead of a slow stage (the open-loop failure mode) + position feedback re-centres each tick + pump tracks real distance ⇒ bead correct at any speed. SMS set to max + brisk accel so `VS` reaches the commanded velocity; velocity clamped to `safety_limits.max_xy_speed`. **Safety:** always stops the stage (`VS 0,0`) on every exit (abort/pause/ZP-drop/stale/runaway/completion/exception via `finally`); a sustained cross-track error (`>_VEL_MAX_CROSS_TRACK_MM` for `_VEL_RUNAWAY_TICKS` — e.g. a `VS` sign inversion) trips a **runaway guard** → `RuntimeError` → outer loop ERROR + retract to safe Z. Falls back to the discrete path on a stage without a continuous-velocity cmd (Ludl HLC).
